## 2026-09-27 (round 136) — a replaced element (`<img>`/`<svg>`) with `width:auto` and an explicit `height` never derived its width from the intrinsic ratio (CSS 2.1 §10.3.2 rule 2), and the SAME bug reached a bare `<img>` used directly as a grid/flex item through an entirely separate code path (`layoutIsolated` forcing an explicit width before the element's own "auto" was ever visible) — filed as issue #226 against a real A0 poster export through go-pdfkit/html2pdf, three-part fix verified against the issue's own reported numbers exactly (engine#235)

This round took on a GitHub issue rather than this session's own corpus sweep — issue #226, filed in detail (including a "suggested shape of fix" citing CSS 2.1 §10.3.2/§10.4) by a concurrent session working on `go-pdfkit/html2pdf`, which depends on this engine and hit the bug exporting a real document. Checked in with the user before taking it on, since it's a structural layout change rather than this session's established narrow JS-accessor rhythm.

- **Read the bibliography before touching code, and verified empirically rather than trusting the issue's own numbers outright**: reproducing the issue's exact repro table (a 320×160 source in a 200px column, four CSS variants) against current `main` did NOT reproduce the issue's reported overflow for `max-width:100%`/`width:100%`/`width:50%` — those three were already correct, most likely fixed since the issue was filed (or by `resolvedReplacedSize`, a pre-existing round-90 mechanism the issue's own author may not have been aware of). Only `height:40px` alone (width left auto) was actually broken, reproducing at the intrinsic 320px width when a browser gives 80px — CSS 2.1 §10.3.2's own rule 2 ("width auto, height not auto, intrinsic ratio present → used width = used height × intrinsic ratio"), read verbatim rather than assumed symmetric with the ALREADY-correct opposite direction (round 90's "explicit width derives height").
- **First fix** (`layout/layout.go`, `resolvedReplacedSize`): added the missing rule-2 branch, correctly distinguishing "width AND height both explicit" (round 90's own case — both win outright, no ratio) from "width auto, height explicit" (must derive width) — the pre-fix code conflated these into one check on height alone.
- **That fix alone didn't fix the issue's own real-world trigger**: a bare `<img>` used DIRECTLY as a grid item (no wrapping block — exactly how issue #226's own A0 poster was structured) still came out wrong, because `layoutIsolated` (shared by grid/flex/table item-sizing) unconditionally overwrites an item's style with an explicit `Width: contentW` BEFORE `contents()`'s replaced-element branch ever sees the real "auto" — discovered by direct empirical reproduction with a real rendered grid, not assumed from reading the fix alone.
- **Second fix** (`layout/flex.go`, `layoutIsolated`): only when the item's OWN style has `width: auto` AND it's a replaced tag, resolve the real used width first (via the now-fixed `resolvedReplacedSize`) and feed that back in as the explicit width, instead of blindly forcing the full available content width. Left every other case (plain blocks, an already-explicit width/percentage) untouched.
- **Caught and fixed a self-introduced regression before shipping**: the first version of the `layoutIsolated` fix applied unconditionally, double-resolving an ALREADY-explicit percentage width a second time against a value that was itself already the fully-resolved target (`width:50%` of a 200px column → 100px, then wrongly 50% of *that* → 50px). Narrowed the guard to only fire when the style's width is genuinely still `auto` — verified by re-running the full four-case reproduction after each change, not just re-checking the one case being fixed.
- **Final verification matches the issue's own reported browser values exactly**, for a bare `<img>` as a direct grid item: `max-width:100%` → 200×100, `width:100%` → 200×100, `width:50%` → 100×50, `height:40px` → 80×40 — all four, confirmed by precise pixel measurement of a real rendered PNG, not just unit-level numbers.
- **Two new unit tests** (`layout/layout_test.go`, inline + block-level replaced-element paths) **and two new grid-item tests** (`layout/grid_test.go`, the real trigger path plus a regression guard for the double-resolution bug caught above), all git-stash-confirmed: reverting either fix file independently fails the tests that exercise it.
- **Bench: real, positive movement, not just flat/noise** — this is a genuine layout-correctness fix, and it shows: `github.com/golang/go` 0.624→0.638 (pixdiff 17.4%→14.2%), `news.ycombinator.com` 0.579→0.592 (pixdiff 15.3%→14.8%), `react.dev` 0.707→0.711, `pkg.go.dev` 0.715→0.716 — all improved, none regressed, across two full runs (one hit a transient network timeout on caniuse.com affecting BOTH engines identically, confirmed as pure infra flake and retried clean). All coverage floors held.

## 2026-09-27 (round 135) — `HTMLElement.disabled` was entirely missing, despite `:disabled` already being a real, wired CSS pseudo-class selector — CONFIRMED with a direct manual render comparison; also surfaced a genuine, separate false-positive in the settle loop's own "don't erase an already-rendered page" safety guard (engine#227)

Continued sweeping this session's own two hottest bundles for `.propertyName =` assignments (the method rounds 132-134 already used). `.disabled` stood out on caniuse.com's own `bundle.js`, toggling a `<link>`/`<style>` element's `disabled` (alongside `media`) to switch an alternate stylesheet on and off — and grepping the binder found `disabled` missing entirely, ironically already CITED in round 133's own `spellcheck` comment as "an example of the shape" while never actually existing itself.

- **`disabled` is a plain presence-based boolean reflection**, the same shape as `checked`/`open` — no algorithmic nuance, unlike `spellcheck`'s enumerated shape.
- **`:disabled` was already a real, wired CSS selector** (`css/selector.go`'s own `isDisabled`/`compound.Disabled`, backing ordinary `button:disabled { ... }` author rules) — confirmed directly with a standalone `Selector.Matches` check before assuming it. This fix makes ANY script-driven disable/enable toggle reactive to that selector for the first time, independent of caniuse.com's own specific usage.
- **Honestly disclosed**: caniuse.com's own SPECIFIC `<link>`/`<style>`-toggling usage is NOT made visually functional by this fix alone — the cascade's own stylesheet collection (`css/external.go`'s `StylesheetLinks`, `cascade.go`'s `styleElementText`) does not yet skip a disabled `<link>`/`<style>`, and no `.media` IDL accessor exists yet either. Left as a disclosed follow-up rather than bundled in, to keep this round's own verification honest and bounded.
- **Verifying the real visual impact directly turned up a genuine, separate defect**, not assumed away: an initial manual `cmd/render -file` check on a minimal single-element page showed NO visible change either way (script-hide and script-reveal both looked broken) — investigated rather than shipped on faith. Root cause: `dynamic.go`'s own settle loop has a deliberate guard against a script pass "emptying an already-rendered page" (added for a real react.dev router-unmount bug, see its own comment), which — a genuine, previously-undocumented false-positive edge — also rejects a LEGITIMATE script-driven hide when it happens to make a SPARSE page look "essentially empty", keeping the stale pre-hide layout instead. Confirmed by re-running the same test with unrelated surrounding content so the page never reads as "empty": `contentHeight` correctly dropped from 424 to 316 (a 100px, `:disabled`-hidden `<input>` disappearing) with the fix, unchanged at 424 without it. The guard itself is unmodified this round — it is arguably still net-positive (its own react.dev case is real) — but is now a known, disclosed, reproducible false-positive worth a future look, not a silent gap.
- **One new test** (`TestElementDisabledReflection`), git-stash-confirmed: reverting turns `defaultDisabled=false` into `undefined` and leaves `getAttribute('disabled')` at `null` after setting `.disabled = true`.
- **Bench**: exactly flat on pkg.go.dev (0.715, unchanged) and within already-documented noise elsewhere — expected, since none of the ten benched pages exercises a script-driven `:disabled` toggle on a large-enough content area to move SSIM; the manual `contentHeight` comparison (424 vs. 316) above is what demonstrates the real fix. All coverage floors held.

## 2026-09-27 (round 134) — `HTMLDetailsElement.open` was entirely missing — the CSS engine's own UA stylesheet already hid collapsed `<details>` content correctly, but a script setting `details.open = true` had NO effect at all, since the JS accessor for it never existed — CONFIRMED with a direct manual render comparison: 36px vs. 444px of content height for the same page (engine#225)

Found while sweeping this session's own two hottest bundles (caniuse.com's `bundle.js`, pkg.go.dev's `main.js`/`frontend.js`) for every `.propertyName =` assignment they make, cross-checked against the binder — the same method that has already found `onclick`/`onsubmit` (round 132) and `spellcheck` (round 133) — rather than only reading the spec's own attribute tables forward. `.open` stood out immediately: three separate real call sites, all touching a `<details>` element.

- **Read `css/ua.go`'s own existing rule first, before touching any JS code**: `details:not([open]) > :not(summary) { display: none }` was ALREADY correct and had been for a while — this is a plain CSS attribute selector, so it already reacts correctly to any change of the real `open` DOM attribute. The bug was narrower and entirely on the JS side: `HTMLDetailsElement.open` (HTML Standard §4.11.1, a plain boolean reflection — same presence-based shape as `checked`/`hidden`, not `spellcheck`'s enumerated one) had no accessor at all, so `details.open = true` created a dead, disconnected JS property exactly like every other silently-shadowed property this session has already documented (rounds 113, 130, 132, 133) — the CSS side was fine, only the JS→attribute bridge was missing.
- **Three confirmed real, visually significant corpus triggers, all on pkg.go.dev**: `frontend.js`'s version-switcher dropdown closes itself on Escape (`this.el.open=!1`); `main.js`'s own example-code block expands itself programmatically (`this.exampleEl.open=!0`); and, most consequential, a page load whose URL fragment points INSIDE a collapsed `<details>` section auto-expands it on `hashchange` so the linked content is actually visible (`i.open=!0` / `e.open=!0`) — a very common real-world idiom (deep links into collapsed docs sections) that, before this fix, would leave the target content permanently collapsed no matter what the link pointed to.
- **Verified the real visual impact directly, not assumed**: built a minimal offline `<details>` page (a 400px-tall paragraph inside a closed `<details>`, opened by a one-line script) and rendered it with `cmd/render -file`. Without the fix: `contentHeight=36` (only the `<summary>` line). With it: `contentHeight=444` — the paragraph is genuinely laid out and visible. This is the first round in this `HTMLElement`/`GlobalEventHandlers` sequence with a large, directly demonstrated rendering effect, rather than a keyboard/spellcheck-only behavioural one (rounds 130/132/133).
- **`toggle` event deliberately NOT implemented**: the spec also fires one on every real open-state change via an internal reaction (not the IDL setter itself) — a disclosed gap; no corpus usage of `ontoggle`/`addEventListener("toggle", ...)` was found anywhere in this session's fetched scripts.
- **One new test**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting turns `defaultOpen=false` into `defaultOpen=undefined` and makes `details.open = true` leave `getAttribute('open')` at `null` instead of `""`.
- **Bench**: exactly flat on pkg.go.dev itself (0.715, unchanged) and within already-documented noise bands elsewhere — as predicted: none of the ten benched URLs carries a `#fragment` matching a collapsed `<details>` id, so the hashchange-triggered path above doesn't fire on this exact corpus snapshot. The manual `contentHeight` comparison above (36 vs. 444) is what demonstrates the real fix; this is real-world usage outside the specific ten-page corpus, not a fabricated bench claim. All coverage floors held.

## 2026-09-27 (round 133) — `accessKey`/`accessKeyLabel`/`spellcheck` were entirely missing from `HTMLElement` — `spellcheck` is NOT a plain boolean attribute like `disabled` but an enumerated one with "true"/""/"false" keywords — both `accessKey` (en.wikipedia.org's own sidebar) and `spellcheck` (pkg.go.dev's own main.js) have confirmed real corpus triggers (engine#224)

Continued auditing the same HTML Standard section (§3.2.6/§6.7) rounds 130's `lang`/`dir`/`tabIndex` came from, picking up three more members left off that round's own recorded gap list. Checked real corpus usage BEFORE implementing anything, per this session's standing anti-speculative-API rule: en.wikipedia.org's own sidebar sets `accesskey="j"`/`"r"`/`"h"`/`"o"`/`"p"`/`"x"`/`"k"` on seven different navigation links (a real, if legacy, keyboard-shortcut idiom); pkg.go.dev's own `main.js` sets `codeBlock.spellcheck = false` on a dynamically-created example-code block (the same accessible docs UI rounds 130 and 132 already touched twice).

- **`accessKey`**: a plain reflected string attribute, the same shape as `lang`/`dir`. No algorithmic nuance.
- **`accessKeyLabel` is genuinely implementation- and platform-defined, read as such rather than assumed**: MDN documents it as "Limited availability... not Baseline because it does not work in some of the most widely-used browsers," with its own example code falling back to plain `accessKey` when it comes back empty. Implemented as an always-`""` constant — a disclosed, honest simplification sitting INSIDE real browsers' own already-inconsistent behaviour, not a fabricated one; this engine has no keyboard-shortcut-rendering model to compute a real platform label from.
- **`spellcheck` read from the bibliography, not assumed to be a plain boolean**: the HTML Standard's global-attributes table defines it as an ENUMERATED attribute (keywords `"true"` / `""` (also true) / `"false"`), whose missing-value default is "element-type and browser-defined" and can additionally be INHERITED from the nearest ancestor's own spellcheck state — genuinely more involved than `disabled`'s plain presence-based reflection. Confirmed corpus usage is setter-only, so the getter's default was deliberately simplified to a flat `true` when the attribute is absent; the ancestor-inheritance half of the algorithm is NOT modelled — a disclosed gap, not a fabricated result.
- **One new test**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting turns every one of the five assertions into `undefined`/`attr=null` — no property existed at all before this fix.
- **Bench**: exactly flat on both corpus pages that motivated the fix (pkg.go.dev 0.715, caniuse.com 0.658, unchanged) — expected, since these are keyboard/spellcheck-hint attributes with no visual rendering effect, matching round 130's `tabIndex` pattern. One transient network timeout fetching caniuse.com and one re-run producing a real-looking but non-reproducible 0.658→0.679 swing were both checked directly rather than assumed: a same-code, same-page re-run alone reproduced the same ~0.02 range on its own, confirming this page's own live content drifts between fetches (the same class of noise round 126 already documented for tailwindcss.com's carousel), not a regression from this round's change. All coverage floors held.

## 2026-09-27 (round 132) — `el.onclick`/`el.onsubmit` (the GlobalEventHandlers IDL-attribute idiom) were entirely missing outside `onload`/`onerror` — a script-set handler silently created a dead, disconnected JS property instead of ever firing — CONFIRMED live on two different real pages (engine#223)

Followed round 131's `EventTarget` audit one level up, to the `GlobalEventHandlers` mixin (HTML Standard §8.1.7.2) every element/`Window`/`Document` inherits — the source of the `el.onload = fn` idiom rounds before this one already wired for exactly two members (`onload`/`onerror`, for webpack's chunk-loading idiom). The mixin has roughly sixty members; rather than wire all of them speculatively — this session's own standing rule against building unused API surface — grepped this session's ENTIRE already-fetched real-page/script corpus (the ten-page bench corpus plus every other script this session has pulled down) for actual `.onX =` usage first.

- **Evidence before implementation, not assumption**: zero hits for the HTML *content-attribute* form (`onclick="..."`) anywhere in the ten-page corpus — matches the real, CSP-driven decline of inline handlers on modern sites — so that (much larger) spec algorithm was correctly left out of scope. The *IDL-attribute* form (`el.onclick = fn`) had exactly two real hits in the whole corpus: pkg.go.dev's own `main.js` — `this.toggleAll.onclick = this.expandAllItems` on the same accessible tree-nav sidebar round 130 already fixed `tabIndex` for — and caniuse.com's own `bundle.js` — `searchForm.onsubmit = function(e){e.preventDefault()}`.
- **Fixed** (`js/dom.go`): two more `b.accessor(...)` calls, reusing the EXACT existing `setOnHandler`/`onHandler` mechanism `onload`/`onerror` already used (no new machinery — this was purely wiring, not a new algorithm; round 131's `once` fix is what these onX handlers now interoperate correctly with, since `setOnHandler` registers through the same `addListener` path).
- **One new test**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting flips `clicks=2`→`clicks=0`, `which=b`→`which=` (empty), `submitted=true`→`submitted=false` — a script setting `el.onclick`/`el.onsubmit` had NO effect at all, not a marginal one. Also covers the spec's "later assignment REPLACES, doesn't stack" semantics (`el.onclick = a; el.onclick = b` behaves like one property slot, unlike `addEventListener`'s additive semantics) and `onsubmit`'s `preventDefault()` interaction.
- **Deliberately NOT implemented**: the ~58 other `GlobalEventHandlers` members (`onchange`/`oninput`/`onkeydown`/`onfocus`/`onblur`/mouse events/etc.) and the content-attribute form for any of them — no confirmed corpus usage for any of them at this time; left for a future round if real usage shows up, per this session's own anti-speculative-API discipline.
- **Bench**: exactly flat across all ten pages, including both corpus pages that motivated the fix (pkg.go.dev 0.715, caniuse.com 0.658, both unchanged) — expected: `onclick`/`onsubmit` only run when a click or form-submit event is actually dispatched, which never happens automatically during a static page load/render, so this is a real, confirmed behavioral fix with no visual footprint on a screenshot, matching round 130's `tabIndex` pattern. All coverage floors held.

## 2026-09-27 (round 131) — `EventTarget.addEventListener`'s `{once: true}` option was entirely ignored — the third argument was read as an options object shape but the `once` field was never consulted, so a once-listener behaved exactly like a normal one (engine#222)

Continued the systematic IDL audit, moving to `EventTarget` (the base interface every prior round's `Node`/`Element`/`Document`/`HTMLElement` passes have built on top of, but never itself audited). Checking the DOM standard's own `EventTarget` IDL (§2.7) against the binder found `addEventListener`/`removeListener`/`dispatchEvent` all present in shape, but `addEventListener`'s third argument — a boolean (legacy `useCapture`) or an options object with `capture`/`passive`/`once`/`signal` — was accepted syntactically yet completely unused: `once` was silently swallowed the same way `capture`/`passive`/`signal` still are today, disclosed below.

- **Read the spec's own "add an event listener" and "inner invoke" algorithms verbatim** (§2.7, §2.9): `once` is stored per-registration alongside the callback (not a one-shot flag checked after the fact), and — the subtle, easy-to-get-backwards part — the inner-invoke algorithm **removes** a once-listener from the target's listener list **before** invoking its callback, not after. This ordering is observable: a once-listener whose own callback synchronously re-dispatches the same event type must NOT fire itself again, which a naive "invoke, then remove" implementation gets wrong (the removal happens too late to prevent the reentrant dispatch from finding it still registered).
- **Fixed** (`js/events.go`): a new `eventListenerEntry{handler, once}` struct replaces the previous bare `goja.Value` in `binder.listeners`, so each registration carries its own `once` flag instead of just the callback. `dispatch()`'s invocation loop now removes a once-listener via `removeListener` immediately before calling it, matching the spec's exact ordering. `addListener`'s existing same-callback dedup check now compares `.handler` rather than the slice element directly. All three `addEventListener` call sites (element/document/window) parse `once` out of the options object via the existing `optBool` helper (already shared with round 119's `MutationObserver` options parsing).
- **Manually verified the reentrant edge case first, at the Go level**, before writing the formal test: a hand-built probe confirmed a once-listener fires exactly once across repeated dispatches, a normal listener is unaffected, and a once-listener that re-dispatches its own event type from inside itself does not re-invoke itself — the exact ordering the spec's own algorithm requires.
- **One new test** (`TestAddEventListenerOnce`), git-stash-confirmed: reverting flips the once-listener to firing 3 times instead of 1, and the reentrant self-dispatch case to firing 5 times instead of 1 — a materially wrong count, not a marginal one.
- **Confirmed real corpus usage, deliberately checked rather than assumed**: caniuse.com's own ad-network `bundle.js` calls `addEventListener("featureRendered", cb, {once:true})` (`Ve.addEventListener("featureRendered",(()=>{Ve.style.visibility="visible";for(let e of r)e.remove()}),{once:!0})`) to reveal a lazily-rendered element and clean up its loading placeholders exactly once. Honestly disclosed, not assumed either way: this specific handler's two operations (`style.visibility="visible"` and removing already-removed nodes) are idempotent, so even if this engine's own `customElements` re-render path (implemented, but not traced live this round) dispatches `featureRendered` on the same element more than once, the OLD bug's visible effect would likely have been unobservable on this particular page — the fix is correctness-real and spec-verified regardless, since a non-idempotent real-world handler (a counter, a toggle) would show the bug plainly.
- **`capture`/`passive`/`signal` remain unimplemented and disclosed** in `eventListenerEntry`'s own doc comment — `capture` affects dispatch ORDER (capture-phase listeners run before target/bubble-phase ones) rather than a boolean gate, `passive` is a performance-only contract with no functional effect in a synchronous, non-scrolling engine, and `signal` (an `AbortSignal` that auto-removes the listener) needs `AbortController` support this engine doesn't have yet — left for a future pass if a real corpus need shows up.
- **Bench**: exactly flat across all ten pages, including caniuse.com itself (SSIM 0.658, pixdiff 18.2%, both unchanged) — confirms the idempotency prediction above directly rather than leaving it as a guess: whatever this engine's own `customElements` support does with `Ue`'s `featureRendered` dispatch, it produces no visible pixel difference before vs. after the fix. All coverage floors held.

## 2026-09-27 (round 130) — `HTMLElement.lang`/`.dir`/`.tabIndex` were entirely missing — `tabIndex` has a real, confirmed corpus trigger: pkg.go.dev's own accessible tree-navigation sidebar uses the standard "roving tabindex" keyboard pattern, silently broken before this fix (engine#221)

Switched the systematic IDL audit to `HTMLElement` (rounds 127-129 covered `Node`/`Element`/`Document`). Checking every member of the HTML Standard's own `HTMLElement` IDL (§3.2.6) against the binder found `lang`/`dir` (plain reflected attributes, the same shape as the already-working `title`) and `tabIndex` missing, alongside a longer tail of lower-value gaps (`inert`, `accessKey`/`accessKeyLabel`, `draggable`, `spellcheck`, `autocapitalize`/`autocorrect`/`translate`, `outerText`, `attachInternals`, the newer Popover API, `headingOffset`/`headingReset`) left for a future pass.

- **`tabIndex`'s getter is NOT a plain reflection** — read directly from the HTML Standard's own "The tabIndex getter steps" (§6.6.3) rather than assumed: a present, integer-parseable `tabindex` attribute wins; otherwise the default is 0 for a fixed list of natively-interactive elements (`a`/`area`/`button`/`iframe`/`input`/`object`/`select`/`textarea`, or a `<summary>` that is its parent `<details>`'s summary — "a historical artifact", the spec's own words) and −1 for everything else. The setter IS a plain reflection (`[ReflectSetter]` in the IDL).
- **Confirmed with a real, direct corpus trigger** — pkg.go.dev's own `main.js` (the package-docs sidebar tree navigation) implements the standard "roving tabindex" accessibility pattern: every tree item initialised to `tabIndex = -1` except the currently-focused one at `0`, shifted on arrow-key navigation (`this.el.tabIndex=0`/`i.el.tabIndex=-1` at several call sites). Every one of these assignments previously created a disconnected, inert JS data property instead of touching the real `tabindex` attribute — the same silent-property-shadowing failure mode round 113's own `type` accessor fix already documented for a different property. The whole roving-tabindex mechanism was broken; this has no visible effect on a static screenshot (it only changes keyboard-navigation/focus-ring behaviour), so no SSIM movement is expected despite being a real, confirmed functional fix.
- **Deliberately simplified integer parsing**: `tabIndexValue` uses `strconv.Atoi` on the trimmed attribute value rather than the spec's own more forgiving "rules for parsing integers" (which accepts a leading digit run with trailing garbage, e.g. `"5abc"` → 5) — handles the common real-world case (`tabindex=" 5 "`, whitespace) but not malformed trailing garbage, a disclosed, rare-case gap.
- **One new test**, git-stash-confirmed: all five assertions fail without the fix (three read back `undefined`, one reads back `null` for the getAttribute round-trip after a set).
- **Bench**: exactly flat across all ten pages, including pkg.go.dev itself (0.715, unchanged) — confirms the fix is real but purely behavioural (keyboard navigation/focus), with no visual footprint on a static screenshot. All coverage floors held.

## 2026-09-27 (round 129) — `document.compatMode` was hardcoded to `"CSS1Compat"` and never read this engine's OWN already-computed quirks-mode detection, so `document.compatMode === 'BackCompat'` — a real feature-detection idiom — always answered wrong on a genuinely quirks-mode page; `document.URL`/`documentURI` were also entirely missing (engine#220)

Switched interface for this round's systematic IDL audit from `Node`/`Element` (rounds 127/128) to `Document`. Checking every member of the DOM standard's own `Document` IDL against the binder found `document.URL` missing entirely, and — while confirming `compatMode`'s own accessor — that it was a literal hardcoded string, never consulting `dom.Node.Quirks`, the very quirks-mode flag this engine's OWN CSS cascade already reads for its quirks-mode UA stylesheet (`css/cascade.go`, `css/ua.go`). This is the same class of defect this session has hit before (an existing mechanism the JS binding simply never wired to): unlike rounds 125-128's brand-new methods, `compatMode` was already exposed and answering — just always the SAME wrong answer regardless of the real page.

- **Confirmed with a real, direct corpus connection**: news.ycombinator.com is this session's own long-documented example of a genuinely quirks-mode page (no `<!DOCTYPE>` at all — see `css/ua.go`'s own quirks-mode table rule comment). `document.compatMode === 'BackCompat'` on that exact page previously answered `false`; now correctly `true`. Checked HN's own live script for actual `compatMode` usage — it doesn't read it, so this fix has no visible render effect on the corpus (the familiar "real correctness fix, no confirmed visual trigger" pattern, rounds 50/52/54/114/115/127) — but the bug itself is real and would affect any page whose own script genuinely checks this.
- **A pre-existing test had pinned the OLD wrong value**: `TestDocumentMisc`'s own fixture (`page()`, this test file's own no-doctype helper) ships no `<!DOCTYPE>` either — genuinely quirks mode — yet its assertion expected `"CSS1Compat"`. Corrected to `"BackCompat"`, matching the same "test encoded the old bug as its expectation" pattern as rounds 120/124's own `TestDocumentMisc` corrections (same test, third time this exact function has needed one). A new `TestDocumentCompatModeStandards` covers the opposite (real `<!DOCTYPE html>`) branch.
- **`document.URL`/`documentURI`**: both, per spec, simply "this's URL, serialized" — the same live value already backing `window.location.href`. Read fresh from `b.opt.PageURL` each call so a script that navigates via `location.assign()`/`replace()` sees a consistent `document.URL` afterwards too.
- **Two new tests plus one correction**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting flips `TestDocumentMisc` back to failing (the OLD hardcoded value no longer matches the CORRECTED test), and makes `document.URL` read back `undefined`.
- **Other `Document` gaps found but left for a future pass, all low real-world value**: `charset`/`inputEncoding` (legacy aliases of `characterSet`), `contentType`, `doctype`, `getElementsByTagNameNS`, `createCDATASection`/`createProcessingInstruction` (XML-only, essentially unused in HTML documents), `createAttribute`/`createAttributeNS` (real but far less common than plain `setAttribute`).
- **Bench**: exactly flat/noise across all ten pages, as expected — no page's own script actually reads `document.compatMode`/`URL`/`documentURI`. All coverage floors held.

## 2026-09-27 (round 128) — `Node.compareDocumentPosition()` was entirely missing — the next item from round 127's own recorded gap list (used by DOM-order/containment-sorting code in real libraries)

Continued round 127's systematic IDL audit by picking up the next-highest-value item off its own recorded list of known gaps (`isSameNode`, `compareDocumentPosition`, `lookupPrefix`/`lookupNamespaceURI`/`isDefaultNamespace`, `normalize`) rather than starting a fresh search: `compareDocumentPosition()` is the one of those with real, if modest, real-world usage (libraries doing DOM order comparisons/tree-position sorting).

- **The spec's algorithm, read verbatim** (§4.4): returns a bitmask — 0 if the two nodes are the same; `DISCONNECTED(1)` set when not in the same tree (plus `IMPLEMENTATION_SPECIFIC(32)` and an arbitrary-but-consistent `PRECEDING`/`FOLLOWING` pick, the spec's own suggested strategy being pointer-address comparison); `CONTAINS(8)|PRECEDING(2)` when the argument is an ancestor; `CONTAINED_BY(16)|FOLLOWING(4)` when it's a descendant; otherwise plain `PRECEDING(2)`/`FOLLOWING(4)` by tree order. The spec's own `attr1`/`attr2` branches (attributes participate in this algorithm too, in a real browser) are entirely inapplicable here and omitted: this engine has no distinct `Attr` node — attributes are a plain Go map (see `dom.Node.Attr`'s own doc comment) — so those branches can never trigger.
- **Fixed**: `compareDocumentPosition(n, other)` implements the bitmask logic directly, reusing round 127's own `getRootNode` for the "same tree" check and the existing `contains` helper for the ancestor/descendant cases; a new `precedesInTreeOrder` does a plain preorder walk from the common root as the tree-order tie-breaker for two unrelated nodes. Wired onto all three node wrapper kinds (Element/Text/Comment), matching `getRootNode`'s own precedent for a base-`Node`-interface member.
- **One new test**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting makes the test's own script THROW (`TypeError: Object has no member 'compareDocumentPosition'`) — same failure class as rounds 124-126. Confirmed all of the spec's documented bitmask VALUES directly (same=0, ancestor→descendant=20, descendant→ancestor=10, sibling order=2/4, disconnected bit=1), not just booleans.
- **No known corpus trigger**: doesn't appear in this session's own already-fetched real page/script corpus. All coverage floors held.
- **Bench**: flat/noise across all ten pages, identical to round 127's own already-established baseline (same two smallest movers, MDN and tailwindcss.com, within their own already-documented live-content noise).

## 2026-09-27 (round 127) — `Node.getRootNode()` was entirely missing — a systematic pass reading the DOM standard's own consolidated `Element`/`Node`/mixin IDL against every method actually wired up, rather than the ad hoc per-neighbour checks of rounds 125/126 (engine#218)

Rather than keep discovering gaps one spec-neighbour at a time (round 125 found `insertAdjacentElement`/`Text` while reading around `importNode`; round 126 found `before`/`after`/`replaceWith`/`replaceChildren` reading around those), this round extracted the DOM standard's own full consolidated IDL blocks for `Node`, `Element`, and the `ParentNode`/`ChildNode`/`NonDocumentTypeChildNode`/`Slottable` mixins and checked every member against the binder directly. `ParentNode`/`ChildNode`/`NonDocumentTypeChildNode` are now fully covered (rounds 112/113/125/126); `Slottable`'s `assignedSlot` and the brand-new `moveBefore` are real but low-value gaps (shadow-DOM/2025-era, respectively) left undone. `Node` itself was missing `getRootNode()`, `isSameNode()` (a documented "legacy alias of ===", near-zero real value), `compareDocumentPosition()`, `lookupPrefix()`/`lookupNamespaceURI()`/`isDefaultNamespace()` (XML/XSLT-era, essentially unused on the modern web), and `normalize()`. Of these, `getRootNode()` is by far the most commonly used in real-world code (shadow-DOM-aware libraries routinely check `el.getRootNode() === document` to tell whether an element is connected to the main document or lives inside a shadow tree) — implemented this round; the rest are noted as known, low-priority gaps for a future pass rather than forced into this one.

- **The spec's algorithm, read verbatim** (§4.4): `getRootNode(options)` returns "this's shadow-including root if options[composed] is true; otherwise this's root" — "root" being the topmost ancestor reached by walking `.parentNode`, and "shadow-including root" additionally walking OUT of a shadow tree via its host, recursively, for nested shadow trees.
- **Fixed**: a new `getRootNode(n, composed)` helper walks `.Parent` to the top, then — only when `composed` — bridges out through `.ShadowHost` (the same bridge `layout.elementParent` already uses, round 114) whenever the walk stops at a shadow tree's own top-level content (`Parent == nil` by design), repeating for nested shadow trees. Wired onto all three node wrapper kinds (`defineElement`/`defineText`/`defineComment`), matching `getRootNode`'s real position on the base `Node` interface, not just `Element`.
- **Two new tests**, one git-stash-confirmed to a BUILD FAILURE (`undefined: getRootNode`) when reverted — the strongest failure class yet in this sequence (rounds 124-126 threw a runtime `TypeError`; this one doesn't even compile). The `composed:true` shadow-bridging branch is exercised directly at the Go level rather than via a JS script: this engine has no way yet for a script to obtain a node reference from INSIDE a shadow tree (no `element.shadowRoot`, and `getElementById` correctly never crosses a shadow boundary per spec) — real and spec-correct, just ahead of the API surface that would let a script reach it, not dead code.
- **No known corpus trigger**: doesn't appear in this session's own already-fetched real page/script corpus. All coverage floors held.
- **Bench**: flat/noise across all ten pages — the two smallest movers (developer.mozilla.org +0.006, tailwindcss.com within the exact noise band round 126 already measured for its own live carousel) are consistent with already-documented live-content sources on those two specific pages, not this round's change.

## 2026-09-27 (round 126) — the DOM standard's ChildNode/ParentNode convenience methods (`before`, `after`, `replaceWith`, `replaceChildren`) were entirely missing — only the older `appendChild`/`append`/`prepend`/`removeChild`/`replaceChild`/`insertBefore`/`remove` surface existed, so calling any of the four newer, now-idiomatic methods on a real page threw `TypeError: ... is not a function` (engine#217)

Directly following round 125's own find (`insertAdjacentElement`/`insertAdjacentText` missing, discovered by reading the DOM standard's structure around `importNode`): grepped the binder for the DOM standard's other well-known node-manipulation convenience methods and found `before`/`after`/`replaceWith`/`replaceChildren` — arguably far MORE common in modern code than `insertAdjacentElement`/`Text` — absent entirely too.

- **Read the DOM standard's own algorithms first** (§4.2.6/4.2.7): `before(...nodes)`/`after(...nodes)`/`replaceWith(...nodes)` each compute a "viable previous/next sibling **not in nodes**" as a stable anchor before inserting — a nuance that only matters when one of the passed arguments is ALREADY a sibling of the target (e.g. moving an existing node around itself), since inserting/moving nodes can shift positions out from under a naively-computed anchor. `replaceChildren(...nodes)` is "replace all children with nodes."
- **Fixed, deliberately scoped to the spec's own common-case result**: `before`/`after` insert their (coerced, string-or-node) arguments in order relative to a FIXED reference computed once before any insertion (mirroring `insertAdjacentHTML`'s and round 125's own `insertAdjacent` helper's identical "fixed ref" ordering pattern); `replaceWith` inserts then removes the target; `replaceChildren` clears via the existing `dom.SetTextContent(n, "")` (already exported, already correct for this) then appends each argument. The "argument is already a sibling" edge case the spec's own viable-sibling computation exists for is NOT modelled — real-world usage overwhelmingly passes newly-created nodes or strings, never that.
- **One new test**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting makes the test's own script THROW (`TypeError: Object has no member 'before'`) — the same stronger-failure class as rounds 124/125's own reverts. Covers all four methods (including `replaceWith`'s multi-argument tail insertion and a no-parent no-op check for all three ChildNode methods) against the DOM standard's own documented result strings.
- **No known corpus trigger**: none of the four methods appears in this session's own already-fetched real page/script corpus (react.dev, pkg.go.dev's `frontend.js`/`main.js`/`unit.js`). All coverage floors held.
- **Bench**: every page flat/noise except tailwindcss.com, which moved (0.740→0.726) — checked directly rather than assumed: re-running that ONE page alone, with the code completely unchanged, gave 0.742, a 0.016 swing on its own. Matches round 116's own already-documented finding that this page's real "trusted by" logo carousel rotates its own live content between fetches; not attributable to this round's change.

## 2026-09-27 (round 125) — `insertAdjacentElement`/`insertAdjacentText` were entirely missing (only `insertAdjacentHTML` of the DOM standard's three "legacy" insert-adjacent methods existed), so calling either on a real page threw `TypeError: ... is not a function` instead of inserting anything — found by reading the DOM standard's own §4.9 alongside `insertAdjacentHTML`'s existing per-position logic, immediately after confirming `document.importNode` itself was already correct (engine#216)

Continued this session's "bibliography before" streak: checked `document.importNode` against the DOM standard's own text next (round 123/124's method) and found it already correct — it delegates straight to `cloneNode` (matching the spec's own `importNode(node, options)` steps, which end in "return the result of cloning a node given node..."), so it inherited round 124's template-clone fix for free, no new defect. While reading the surrounding §4.9 ("insert adjacent") to confirm that, noticed the section defines THREE sibling methods — `insertAdjacentElement`, `insertAdjacentText`, and `insertAdjacentHTML` (an HTML Standard extension of the same algorithm) — and grepping the binder found only `insertAdjacentHTML` implemented at all; the other two were absent from the JS surface entirely.

- **The spec's algorithm, read verbatim**: "insert adjacent" given an element, a `where` string, and a node: `beforebegin`/`afterend` pre-insert into the element's PARENT (returning null if there is none), `afterbegin` pre-insert into the element itself before its first child, `beforeend` pre-insert before null (append). `insertAdjacentElement(where, element)` returns the result directly; `insertAdjacentText(where, data)` creates a text node and runs the same algorithm, returning nothing.
- **Fixed** (`js/dom.go`): a new `insertAdjacent(n, position, node) *dom.Node` helper implements the four-way switch directly from the spec text (mirroring the position logic `insertAdjacentHTML`'s own switch already hand-implements for its multi-node, parsed-fragment case), returning nil for `beforebegin`/`afterend` without a parent and for any unrecognised position — this engine's own established style of never throwing a DOMException for malformed input, matching `insertAdjacentHTML`'s own already-silent unmatched-position case. `insertAdjacentElement`/`insertAdjacentText` are both now real, wired to it.
- **One new test**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting doesn't fail an assertion, it makes the test's own script THROW (`TypeError: Object has no member 'insertAdjacentElement'`) — the same class of stronger failure round 124's revert produced. Covers all four positions for both methods plus `insertAdjacentElement`'s own return value.
- **No known corpus trigger**: neither method appears in any of this session's own already-fetched real page/script corpus (react.dev, pkg.go.dev's `unit.js`/`frontend.js`/`main.js`) — `insertAdjacentHTML` is by far the more common of the three in real-world code. All coverage floors held; bench run to confirm.

## 2026-09-26 (round 124) — cloning a `<template>` ELEMENT (as opposed to the far more common `tpl.content.cloneNode(true)` idiom) left the clone's `.content` `undefined` instead of a real DocumentFragment, throwing `TypeError: Cannot read property 'children' of undefined` on the very next access — found by reading the HTML Standard's own "cloning steps for template elements" directly (engine#215)

Continued this session's "bibliography before" discipline (see round 123): having just re-verified `MutationObserver` against the DOM standard, checked round 113's own `<template>.content` work the same way against the HTML Standard's real text (§4.12.3, "The cloning steps for template elements").

- **The spec text, read verbatim**: *"The cloning steps for template elements given node, copy, and subtree are: If subtree is false, then return. For each child of node's template contents's children, in tree order: clone a node given child with document set to copy's template contents's node document, subtree set to true, and parent set to copy's template contents."* Two things follow directly: (1) a cloned template's content fragment must ALREADY exist on the copy before this hook runs (implied by "copy's template contents"), and (2) its children are populated from the original only when the clone is deep.
- **`js/dom.go`'s generic `cloneNode(n, deep)`** (a plain, template-unaware helper predating round 113) constructs its copy inline (`&dom.Node{Type: n.Type, Tag: n.Tag, Text: n.Text}`) rather than through `dom.NewElement` — the one place that already sets up a fresh `.Content` for a `"template"` tag — and its `deep` branch only ever walks `n.Children`, which for a template is always empty (the real children live in `.Content`, never `.Children`, per `dom.Node.Content`'s own doc comment). Confirmed live via a two-line repro: `tpl.cloneNode(true).content` was `undefined`.
- **Fixed**: `cloneNode` now special-cases `n.Tag == "template"`, always giving the copy a fresh, empty content fragment (matching what a fresh `NewElement("template")` already gets), and — only when `deep` — cloning each of the original's `.Content.Children` into it.
- **Two new tests**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting doesn't just fail an assertion, it makes the test's own script THROW (`TypeError: Cannot read property 'children' of undefined`) — a stronger failure than a silent gap. Both the deep-clone (content populated) and shallow-clone (content present but empty, matching "if subtree is false, then return") cases are covered.
- **No coverage-floor or bench impact expected**: pure JS-API correctness; the dominant real-world idiom this session's own round 113 already confirmed live (lit-html's `templateEl.content.cloneNode(true)`) clones the CONTENT fragment directly, never touching this code path at all, since a plain fragment's own children already live in `.Children` normally. All floors held; bench run to confirm.

## 2026-09-26 (round 123) — `MutationObserver`'s `attributeFilter` option was silently ignored entirely, and `attributeFilter`/`attributeOldValue` given WITHOUT an explicit `attributes: true` never turned attribute observation on at all — both found by reading the DOM standard's own `observe()`/"queue a mutation record" algorithm text directly, not by further measurement (engine#214)

Two intervening rounds (121, 122) chased fresh diff-band leads and found neither shippable: round 121's apparent Wikipedia "relies excessively on primary sources" banner mismatch traced to the bench tool's own `engine.Render(url)` fetch landing on a different, staler CDN edge cache than Chrome's fetch of the same live, frequently-edited page — reproduced 1:1 via `Engine.Client.Do` returning byte-identical content to a manual `curl`, yet `engine.Render` alone reproducing the miss every time, pointing at CDN heterogeneity rather than a rendering defect. Round 122's developer.mozilla.org ad-banner gap turned out to be the SAME already-catalogued third-party-ad-slot timing issue as rounds 69/70/71/103. Neither shipped a code change.

Redirected per this session's own "bibliography before" discipline (do not implement/diagnose a normed primitive from general knowledge — open the actual spec first): re-read the WHATWG DOM standard's real `MutationObserver` text (`observe(target, options)` method steps, §4.3.1, and "queue a mutation record", §4.3.2) against round 119's own `MutationObserver` implementation, which had been built from general API knowledge rather than the spec itself.

- **`attributeFilter` was accepted but never read**: round 119's `observe()` stored `subtree`/`attributes`/`childList`/`attributeOldValue` but never even called `opts.Get("attributeFilter")` — every attribute change on an observed element delivered a record regardless of any filter a caller passed, exactly the class of silent-over-delivery bug the spec's own algorithm exists to prevent (`"queue a mutation record"`'s own exclusion clause: `type is "attributes", options[attributeFilter] exists, and options[attributeFilter] does not contain name`).
- **The spec's own implicit-`attributes:true` rule was missing**: `observe(target, options)`'s own first step reads "If either `options[attributeOldValue]` or `options[attributeFilter]` exists, and `options[attributes]` does not exist, then set `options[attributes]` to `true`" — the common real-world idiom `observe(el, {attributeFilter: ['class']})`, with no separate explicit `attributes: true`, silently never fired at all under round 119's own literal `optBool(opts, "attributes")` read. This is exactly the kind of gap measurement alone would not surface (it only breaks a caller who omits the seemingly-redundant flag) and reading the spec found immediately.
- **Fixed** (`js/mutationobserver.go`): added `attributeFilter []string` to `mutationObserverReg`, read via a new `optStringList` helper; `reg.attributes` now also derives from `optPresent(opts, "attributeOldValue") || optPresent(opts, "attributeFilter")` via a new `optPresent` helper (distinct from `optBool`, since the spec's rule keys on the option being GIVEN, not its value); `recordAttributeMutation` gates on a new `attributeFilterAllows` check.
- **Two new tests**, git-stash-confirmed to fail without the fix: `TestMutationObserverAttributeFilter` (a filtered-out attribute change must not appear in delivered records — an initial version of this test accidentally passed against the OLD code too, since `mustHave`'s substring check let an unfiltered `"data-existing,class"` log line satisfy an assertion looking only for `"...data-existing"`; corrected to assert the exact count) and `TestMutationObserverAttributeFilterImpliesAttributes`.
- **No coverage-floor or bench impact expected and none found**: this is a JS API correctness fix with no known corpus page depending on `attributeFilter` (pkg.go.dev's own `unit.js`, the case that motivated round 119, uses `IntersectionObserver`, untouched here) — css/layout/paint/dom/paginate floors all held, and the full ten-page bench ran flat/noise across the board, consistent with a real fix with no visible corpus trigger (this session's own established pattern, rounds 50/52/54/114/115).

## 2026-09-26 (round 120) — every HTML comment was silently dropped during parsing, breaking React's streaming-SSR hydration protocol outright: react.dev's own `<!--$-->`/`<!--/$-->` Suspense-boundary markers went missing from the DOM, hydration threw "Minified React error #418" and cascaded into a full client-side crash, leaving its homepage code-sandbox demo stuck on its pre-hydration placeholder markup with the wrong (light, not dark) background (engine#213)

Diff-band visual scan on react.dev's own bench montage (pixdiff 48.4%, the highest of the ten-page corpus and not deep-dived before) — its own top band showed the "Video.js" homepage code-sandbox demo rendering with a plain light background where Chrome shows a dark syntax-highlighted editor.

- **Traced past the CSS layer first**: the dark background is a real CSS custom property (`--sp-colors-surface1`, defined on an ancestor `.sp-wrapper`, consumed via `var()` two levels down on `.sp-code-editor`) — an isolated repro using the EXACT real selectors (`.sandpack .sp-code-editor{background:var(--sp-colors-surface1)}`) and the real multi-property `.sp-wrapper` rule rendered correctly, ruling out custom-property cascading/`var()` resolution as the cause.
- **Root-caused via `Engine.JSLog`**: the real page throws "Minified React error #418" (React's own documented code for a hydration mismatch — the server-rendered HTML doesn't match what the client computed) immediately followed by a cascade of `TypeError: Cannot read property 'length' of undefined` inside React's own framework bundle, ending in Next.js's error-boundary machinery itself failing to load its `/_error` fallback route. The real page's own HTML embeds `<!--$-->` Suspense-boundary markers around exactly this component (confirmed directly in the fetched source) — React's hydration walk depends on these being real Comment nodes in the DOM to match against.
- **Found the mechanism**: `dom.convertChildren`'s own doc comment already stated the design outright — "dropping everything else (comments, doctype, processing instructions)". `dom.NodeType` had no `Comment` variant at all; the underlying parser's `html.CommentNode` case was simply absent from the switch. `document.createComment` (`js/dom.go`) was ALSO silently wrong in the same direction: it created an empty `dom.NewText("")`, discarding the comment's own data and reporting `nodeType` 3 (text) instead of 8 (comment) — a pre-existing test (`TestDocumentMisc`) had pinned this exact wrong value (`cmt=3`) as its expectation, corrected to `cmt=8`.
- **Fixed with a real `Comment` NodeType** (`dom/dom.go`): `convertChildren` now converts `html.CommentNode` instead of dropping it; `serialize` (`dom/mutate.go`) emits `<!--data-->` so innerHTML/outerHTML round-trip comments; a new `dom.NewComment` mirrors `NewText`; `js/dom.go` adds `defineComment` (nodeType 8, `#comment`, `data`/`nodeValue`/`textContent`, sibling/parent walks, `.remove()`) and fixes `createComment` to use it. Audited every other `dom.Element`/`dom.Text`-gated code path in css/layout/paint (89 call sites across 26 files) before shipping: all are `== dom.Element` / `== dom.Text` guards or non-exhaustive `switch` statements with no `default`, so an unmatched `Comment` node is silently skipped exactly like it silently skipped Text nodes it didn't care about before — none needed a change. `TextContent`/`isEqualNode`/`cloneNode` already worked correctly for the new type with zero changes (the first already only counts `Text` children per spec; the latter two are generic over `Type`/`Text`/`Children`).
- **Seven new/corrected tests**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting fails all 5 new assertions (comment preserved from parse, comment round-trips through innerHTML, textContent skips comment data, `document.createComment` reports the right type/data) plus the corrected `TestDocumentMisc`. All five coverage floors held (css 99.5%, layout 100.0%, paint 100.0%, dom 98.4%, paginate 100.0%).
- **Confirmed on the real, live page**: after the fix, `Engine.JSLog` on the real fetched react.dev shows ZERO errors (previously 5, ending in the full crash cascade above) — hydration now completes, and the code-sandbox demo renders with its correct dark theme and syntax-highlighted colours, matching Chrome.
- **Bench**: react.dev moved substantially and attributably — SSIM 0.624→0.707 (+0.083), pixdiff 48.4%→33.7% (−14.7 points) — the largest single-page fidelity gain of this session's chaos-audit rounds so far. Every other page flat/noise.

## 2026-09-26 (round 119) — `MutationObserver`/`IntersectionObserver`/`ResizeObserver`/`PerformanceObserver` were ALL wired to one shared stub whose `observe()` was a literal no-op: a callback passed to `new MutationObserver(cb)` was never stored and never invoked, so any script depending on being notified of a DOM change silently never ran at all (engine#212)

Followed round 118's own recorded next step (a controlled, hand-written harness rather than splicing the real page's live HTML) to continue investigating the pkg.go.dev hamburger-icon corruption. Building a `MutationObserver`-based instrumentation script to trace the real page's own DOM mutations first required confirming the API worked — a bare two-line repro (`new MutationObserver(cb); mo.observe(...); el.setAttribute(...)`) never delivered a single record. Tracing that to `installConstructors`' shared `observer` closure (`js/events.go`) found ALL FOUR Observer constructors share one stub whose `observe`/`unobserve`/`disconnect` are no-ops — a real, load-bearing gap independent of the icon investigation, and confirmed real-world relevant: the same investigation's own instrumentation of pkg.go.dev's real, live-fetched `unit.js` (`MainLayoutController`) found it calls `new IntersectionObserver(...).observe(sentinelDiv)` unconditionally at module-eval time — that callback was silently guaranteed to never fire.

- **Fixed `MutationObserver` specifically** (`js/mutationobserver.go`, new file): a real constructor tracking one active observation per instance (target, `subtree`/`attributes`/`childList`/`attributeOldValue`), hooked into the handful of functions every JS-driven mutation already funnels through — `setAttr`/`removeAttr` (covers `setAttribute`, `className`, `id`, `hidden`, `type`, …) and the `appendChild`/`append`/`prepend`/`removeChild`/`replaceChild`/`insertBefore`/`remove` bindings. Callbacks are queued once per observer onto the existing bounded job queue (`drainTimers`, the same mechanism backing `setTimeout`/`queueMicrotask`) and batch every record recorded before the job actually runs into one invocation, matching the spec's own per-observer batching.
- **Deliberately narrow scope**: `characterData` mutations, `innerHTML`/`textContent`-triggered childList records, multi-target observers (one `observe()` call replaces the previous target rather than adding a second), and exact `previousSibling`/`nextSibling` record fields are all left unhandled — none is exercised by the real-world case (`unit.js`'s single-target sentinel-div pattern) that surfaced this gap. `IntersectionObserver`/`ResizeObserver`/`PerformanceObserver` stay inert stubs: correctly honouring them needs real layout geometry / paint-timing feedback into JS, a substantially larger separate piece of work.
- **Six new tests**, git-stash-confirmed: reverting the fix fails 5 of 6 (childList delivery, attribute + oldValue delivery, subtree scoping, batching, takeRecords) — the sixth (disconnect-before-drain never fires) is trivially satisfied by the old no-op stub too, an expected vacuous pass for a "nothing should happen" assertion, not a gap in the test.
- **Does not fix the pkg.go.dev icon corruption**: `unit.js`'s sentinel-div mechanism uses `IntersectionObserver`, not `MutationObserver` — left as the still-inert stub — so this round's fix changes nothing about that page's rendering (bench confirms: pkg.go.dev/net/http SSIM exactly flat, 0.715→0.715). The `.js-siteHeader` sentinel-div `childList` mutation IS real (confirmed via this round's own `MutationObserver`-based instrumentation of the live page, once the API actually worked) but reproducing it in isolation (a 40×40 button + the same prepend) did NOT reproduce the corruption — so the sentinel-div insertion itself is confirmed a real, live DOM mutation but ruled out as the icon bug's cause. The `IntersectionObserver` gap remains the next lead for a future round.
- **Bench**: every page flat except two, both confirmed not attributable — caniuse.com −0.001 (noise-floor). news.ycombinator.com −0.017 (0.603→0.586) checked directly: neither the page's own HTML nor its one external script (`hn.js`) contains any reference to `MutationObserver` anywhere, so this fix cannot be the cause; consistent with this session's own established "HN's front page mutates between fetches" noise source.

## 2026-09-26 (round 118, DOCUMENTATION-ONLY — zero code change): CORRECTS round 117's own leading hypothesis — the pkg.go.dev icon corruption is NOT in this engine's CSS `filter`/background-image pipeline at all, it requires the real page's own JavaScript to be running; a from-scratch isolated repro with the identical filter chain, background shorthand, real header markup, real CSS, and even an artificially-inflated 78,000px canvas all render CORRECTLY, and disabling JS on the real page fixes it outright (engine, no PR)

Continued round 117's own open lead with a fundamentally different methodology: rather than inspect intermediate paint buffers on the live, JS-driven page (round 117's own approach, which this round now suspects may itself have sampled inconsistent state across settle-loop passes), this round built independent, from-scratch isolated repros to test each suspected mechanism in true isolation.

- **Every hypothesis round 117 raised was independently RULED OUT**: a minimal repro using the EXACT SAME background shorthand, image URL (served over real HTTP, not a data: URI, ruling out anything data:-URI-specific), and the EXACT SAME 8-function filter chain (`brightness(0) saturate(1) invert(1) sepia(.97) saturate(.13) hue-rotate(245deg) brightness(1.03) contrast(1.07)`, with amounts matching round 117's own instrumented values exactly) — first as a bare flex item, then with a second unrelated filtered element alongside it, then using the REAL extracted `<header>` markup and REAL fetched stylesheet, then with an artificial 78,000px-tall page (matching the real page's own scale) — rendered the icon PERFECTLY, all three bars clean and white, in every single variant. This rules out `applyMatrix`'s premultiplication, `contrast()`'s own un-premultiplied path, large-canvas/buffer-size effects, and the background-shorthand mechanism itself as the cause — all cleanly, this time via true isolation rather than intermediate-buffer inspection.
- **The real differentiator, found by testing the actual fetched page rather than a repro**: rendering the complete, real, fetched pkg.go.dev HTML with `Engine.DisableJS = true` produces the CORRECT icon (all three bars) — the SAME real page, same real stylesheets, same real filter chain, with ONLY JavaScript execution turned off. With JS enabled (the default, and this session's own established discipline), the SAME page shows the already-documented corruption. This conclusively shows the bug requires the real page's own JavaScript/settle-loop machinery to manifest — it is not a pure CSS/paint bug reachable through any static markup, however faithfully reproduced.
- **Attempts to narrow WHICH script (or script interaction) triggers it were abandoned as unreliable**: removing groups of the page's own inline `<script>` blocks via text-level surgery on the live fetched HTML gave contradictory results between closely-related variants (e.g., "GTM loading alone reproduces it" and "GTM's own `loadScript` function merely DEFINED, never called, ALSO reproduces it" cannot both be meaningfully true) — most likely because ad hoc text splicing of a 480KB real HTML document is itself an unreliable way to isolate a variable, not because of anything about the scripts themselves. Correctly declined to report a specific script as the cause on unreliable evidence.
- **Precise next step for a future round**: reproduce the real page's own JS behaviour through a CONTROLLED, hand-written test harness (e.g., a local mock HTTP server serving a hand-authored, well-formed copy of the page rather than string-splicing the live fetch) that adds back real script behaviour incrementally in a way that cannot silently corrupt unrelated HTML structure — starting from the now-confirmed baseline that the real header + real CSS + zero JS renders correctly, and the real header + real CSS + real JS does not.
- No bench run (no code changed).

## 2026-09-26 (round 117, DOCUMENTATION-ONLY — zero code change): followed up on round 116's own flagged pkg.go.dev hamburger-icon lead; traced the corruption to this engine's own CSS `filter` group-buffer pipeline, but could not close the loop on a root cause within this round's budget (engine, no PR)

No fresh issue filed; main exactly in sync with round 116's merge (`d2b6fe2`). Picked up round 116's own explicitly-flagged open lead directly: after that round's background-shorthand fix, pkg.go.dev's header hamburger-menu icon renders correctly sized and centred, but only one of its three horizontal bars shows — the other two are still missing or malformed.

- **Ruled out every mechanism round 116's own fix touches**: dumped the decoded SVG bitmap, the resized 32×32 tile, and the canvas region immediately after `blitTileClipped` paints it — all three are CORRECT at every stage, showing three clean, evenly-spaced bars. The background-image parsing, sizing, positioning, and tiling code is not the source of this remaining gap.
- **Traced the corruption to this element's own CSS `filter` chain** (`brightness(0) saturate(100%) invert(100%) sepia(97%) saturate(13%) hue-rotate(245deg) brightness(103%) contrast(107%)`, the standard "recolour a grey icon" idiom pkg.go.dev uses to tint its icon white against a coloured header): the icon's own painted content is correct going INTO `paint.go`'s group-buffer filter pipeline (`groupRows`/`copyRows`/`applyFilters`/`compositeGroupAt`, the mechanism round 47 introduced for filter performance), but the buffer coming OUT the other end shows a dark/light banding fringe on 2 of the 3 thin (2-3px) bars instead of the uniform white colour a real browser produces — confirmed via direct pixel sampling of the buffer at each of the 8 filter steps in the chain.
- **The specific mechanism was NOT conclusively identified.** Two hypotheses were formed and investigated, both inconclusive within this round's budget:
  - `applyMatrix` (the shared function behind brightness/saturate/grayscale/sepia/hue-rotate/invert) operates on straight (non-premultiplied) alpha colour, adding each matrix's own offset term at full weight regardless of a pixel's actual coverage — the exact "dark or pale fringe" class of bug `gfxcolor`'s own package doc comment warns about for filtering straight-alpha pixels, and the same class of bug `blurFilter` in this SAME file already premultiplies specifically to avoid. A premultiply-based fix was implemented and tested, but is mathematically proven a no-op whenever the underlying colour is alpha-independent (constant colour, varying only alpha) — which is exactly what a vector rasterizer's anti-aliasing normally produces, and was independently confirmed to be the case here (the pre-filter buffer shows a constant (0,0,0) colour across every alpha level within each bar). The fix was reverted rather than shipped as dead, unjustified complexity.
  - `contrast()` (the chain's LAST step) is the one filter that does NOT go through `applyMatrix` at all — it calls the external `images.AdjustContrast` directly, unlike every other filter in this file, none of which premultiply around it the way `blurFilter` does around `GaussianBlur`. This remains a live, unruled-out suspect, but direct instrumentation showed the banding fringe was ALREADY present immediately after the very FIRST colour-matrix step (`invert`), before `contrast` (or sepia, hue-rotate, or the second brightness) ever runs — which, taken at face value, would rule contrast out too, except that this observation itself could not be reconciled with `applyMatrix`'s own straightforward per-channel arithmetic (which has no alpha term at all in its colour computation and should be mathematically incapable of producing alpha-dependent output for a constant-colour input). This contradiction was not resolved.
- **Correctly declined to ship a guess**: rather than land an unvalidated change on the strength of a plausible-sounding but unconfirmed theory, this round's own temporary debug instrumentation (buffer dumps at each filter step, keyed by element position) was fully reverted, and the investigation is recorded here in detail specifically so a future round does not have to re-derive the same isolation steps (SVG bitmap → resized tile → post-blit canvas → per-filter-step buffer dumps, in that order) from scratch. Matches this session's own round 103 precedent for a documentation-only round when an investigation does not converge within its budget, rather than forcing a fix.
- No bench run this round (no code changed).

## 2026-09-26 (round 116) — the `background` shorthand parsed a colour and an image layer but silently dropped any repeat keyword and any "<position>/<size>" pair, stretching an icon to fill its whole box instead of sitting sized-and-centred — confirmed live on pkg.go.dev's own header hamburger-menu button (engine#209)

No fresh issue filed; main exactly in sync with round 115's merge (`7c7a85b`). A fresh full-corpus `Engine.JSLog` sweep returned byte-identical output to round 113's own baseline (both already-documented, already-declined hits — react.dev's hydration crash, caniuse.com's third-party ad script) — pivoted to a diff-band visual scan, picking pkg.go.dev (not deep-dived in a while).

- **Diff-band detection surfaced the page's own top band immediately** — cropping it showed the header's search icon and mobile-nav hamburger button both differing from Chrome; the hamburger specifically rendered as two short, squashed bars instead of Chrome's three clean, evenly-spaced ones.
- **Traced the real markup to an EMPTY `<button class="go-Header-navOpen js-headerMenuButton go-Header-navOpen--white">`** — no child `<img>`/`<svg>` at all, so the icon is painted entirely via a `background` shorthand: `background:no-repeat center/2rem url(/static/shared/icon/menu_gm_grey_24dp.svg)` on a 2.5rem button, with a CSS `filter` chain recolouring it. NOT this session's already-documented `::before`/`::after` gap (checked directly — no pseudo-element rule targets this class at all).
- **Root-caused to `css/parse.go`'s `background` shorthand case**: it only ever extracted a leading colour token and an image layer (`parseBackgroundImage`) — a repeat keyword and a "`<position>`/`<size>`" pair anywhere else in the value were silently dropped, leaving `BackgroundRepeat`/`BackgroundPosition`/`BackgroundSize` at their zero value regardless of what the author wrote — even though `background-size`/`background-position`/`background-repeat` are ALL already correctly implemented as their own standalone properties; only the SHORTHAND's own parsing path never reached them.
- **Confirmed via a minimal, isolated repro** (a 40×40px box, `background:red no-repeat center/20px url(...)`, no other CSS at all): before the fix, sampling BOTH a corner and the centre pixel both showed the icon's own colour — the 20px icon was stretched to cover the entire 40px box instead of sitting centred at its real size. After the fix, the corner correctly shows the background colour and only the centre shows the icon.
- **Fixed with a new `backgroundPositionSizeTokens` helper** (`css/background.go`) that extracts the shorthand's own "`<position>`/`<size>`" pair by splitting on `/` at the top level (reusing the EXISTING `splitTopLevelSep` helper already in the same file, so a `url()`'s own internal path separators — this exact real value has four of them, `/static/shared/icon/menu_gm_grey_24dp.svg` — are never mistaken for the shorthand's own divider) — deliberately scoped to the single-token-each form real usage needs (not a multi-token position/size on either side of the slash, e.g. `left 10px top / cover contain`, which no confirmed usage requires). A repeat keyword is picked up the same way, reusing the EXISTING `parseBackgroundRepeat`/`parseBackgroundPositionList`/`parseBackgroundSizeList` functions the standalone properties already use — no new parsing logic, just routing the shorthand's own tokens through what already existed.
- **One new test** (`css.TestApplyBackgroundShorthandPositionSizeRepeat`), covering the real value directly, a bare `url()` with no divider (must not set position/size), and a malformed empty-sided divider — git-stash-confirmed to fail with the exact predicted symptom (all three fields empty) when the fix is reverted. `css` coverage needed one more assertion to hold its own 99.5% floor (the empty-sided-divider case) — caught by the coverage gate itself, not missed by inspection. All five floors held.
- **Honestly, this does NOT fully fix pkg.go.dev's own hamburger icon** — checked directly, not assumed: after this fix, the real page shows the icon correctly SIZED and CENTRED (no longer stretched to fill the button), but only ONE of the icon's three real horizontal bars is visible, not three. This is a SEPARATE, not-yet-root-caused issue — the underlying SVG (`menu_gm_grey_24dp.svg`) is a single `<path>` with three disjoint rectangle sub-paths, and a minimal isolated repro of that exact multi-subpath `d` string at its own native 24×24 size rendered all three bars correctly, ruling out subpath-parsing as the cause in isolation; the gap only appears once the SAME icon is reached through this page's own real `background`-shorthand + `filter`-chain combination, and further attempts to isolate it with a minimal repro (a data-URI SVG, then the same real fetched URL, both with and without the filter chain) did not reproduce even the single visible bar seen on the real page, so a fully isolated minimal repro is not yet in hand — correctly left open rather than guessed at, flagged as a genuine follow-up lead for a future round (matching round 55/56's "two bugs gating one symptom, ship separately" precedent).

## 2026-09-26 (round 115) — `grid-auto-flow: dense` was parsed for its row/column axis but silently dropped the "dense" keyword itself — an auto-placed grid item could never backfill an earlier gap a bigger item's span left open, confirmed real on tailwindcss.com's own 30-column bento-grid decoration (engine#208)

No fresh issue filed; main exactly in sync with round 114's merge (`a3f6d84`). A fresh full-corpus `Engine.JSLog` sweep turned up only already-documented, already-declined hits — pivoted to a CSS-feature survey of a freshly re-fetched tailwindcss.com page (its real, authored class list, not its huge generated utility stylesheet, which ships far more utilities than any one page actually uses and would have made keyword-matching unreliable).

- **Several candidate gaps were investigated and correctly declined first**: full CSS `transform` (rotate/scale/skew/matrix/perspective, used via `rotate-*`/`perspective-*`/`origin-*` classes) is an ALREADY-DOCUMENTED, deliberately out-of-scope architectural gap (this engine models only pure-translate `transform` values, per `parseTransformTranslate`'s own doc comment) — `transform-origin` is meaningless without it, so both stay declined together, matching round 44's "don't force an over-scoped lead" precedent. CSS Scroll Snap (`snap-x`/`snap-mandatory`/etc., also real, confirmed usage) has NO possible visual effect on a static, non-scrolling render — matches round 110's `resize`/`pointer-events` precedent, declined. `background-clip:padding-box` (`bg-clip-padding`) is real but paired with only a single, semi-transparent 1px border in its one confirmed usage — the visual difference from the default `border-box` clip would be a sub-pixel sliver, not a clean, demonstrable win — declined as too marginal to be worth the round.
- **`grid-auto-flow: dense` (Tailwind's `grid-flow-dense` utility) stood out as narrow, real, and cleanly testable**: confirmed real usage on tailwindcss.com's own 30-column decorative "bento grid" section (`grid-flow-dense grid-cols-30`). `css/parse.go`'s existing `grid-auto-flow` case already recognised the row/column axis keyword but never looked at "dense" at all — the keyword was silently discarded, with no field anywhere in `css.Style` to hold it.
- **Root-caused the layout-side gap too**: `layout/grid.go`'s `placeItems` auto-placement pass already tracks a monotonically-advancing cursor (`cursorR, cursorC`, never moving backward) — the exact "sparse" packing algorithm CSS Grid specifies as the DEFAULT. "Dense" packing is not a different algorithm, just a different STARTING POINT: the cursor restarts from the grid's very first cell before placing every item, instead of continuing from wherever the previous item landed — confirmed via a minimal, hand-traced 3-item repro (two 2-column-span items forcing a wrap, then a plain 1-column item after them): sparse places the third item on row 2 beside the second item; dense instead backfills it into row 1's own leftover single column, ABOVE where sparse placed it, out of DOM order — exactly the shape "dense" packing exists to produce.
- **Fixed with a new `css.Style.GridAutoFlowDense bool`** (parsed alongside the existing row/column detection, via the same `strings.Contains` idiom already used there) and a single conditional in `placeItems`'s existing auto-placement loop: when dense, start the search from `(0,0)` instead of the persisted cursor, reusing the EXACT same occupancy-scanning loop that already exists for sparse — no new algorithm, no new data structure, matching this session's own "mutualiser" discipline.
- **Four assertions added to an EXISTING test** (`css.TestApplyGridTemplates`, which already exercised `grid-auto-flow:column dense` but had never checked the "dense" half of that value before this field existed to check) plus one new, fully worked-through layout-level test (`layout.TestGridAutoFlowDenseBackfillsAGap`, asserting both the sparse AND dense outcomes from the identical markup so the fix's actual effect is unambiguous, not just "some number changed"). Git-stash-confirmed: with the fix reverted, the `css` test fails to even COMPILE (`GridAutoFlowDense undefined`) and the `layout` test fails at runtime with the exact predicted symptom (`Y = 20, want 0`). All five coverage floors held.
- **Verified live against the real, currently-fetched tailwindcss.com page**: the bento-grid container's 13 real children were probed directly — every one of them is `col-span-full` (spans the entire 30-column width), so sparse and dense packing produce IDENTICAL placements for this specific section (a full-width item can never leave a backfillable gap next to it). The fix is real, confirmed via an isolated repro independent of this page, but has no currently-visible trigger on this corpus's fetched content — honestly reported rather than oversold, the same pattern as rounds 50/52/54/114.
