## 2026-09-26 (round 114) — a shadow host that generates no Box of its own (a plain `display:inline` custom element, the spec default) never received ANY layout rect at all — `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` reported it as `display:none` regardless of its real, painted shadow content (engine#207)

No fresh issue filed; main exactly in sync with round 113's merge (`5ed76b5`). A fresh full-corpus `Engine.JSLog` sweep turned up only already-documented, already-declined hits (react.dev's own long-characterised hydration crash, round 82; caniuse.com's own third-party `adsbygoogle.js` error) — pivoted to a diff-band-detection visual scan of the bench montage's worst-scoring pages.

- **Two initial leads dead-ended cleanly, both already documented**: developer.mozilla.org's largest diff band traced to its own already-known third-party ad-slot vertical shift (rounds 69/70/71/103); github.com's largest bands traced to the already-known, deliberate `maxModuleEntryScripts` skeleton-loading cap (round 60/110) and a minor, diffuse sub-pixel image-sizing difference not chased further.
- **A third lead, `clip-path` (entirely unimplemented, confirmed real usage on MDN's own `.visually-hidden` sr-only utility class), was investigated and correctly RULED OUT as visually load-bearing**: a minimal repro confirmed this engine's existing `overflow:hidden` + `position:absolute` + `height:1px`/`width:1px` combination already hides sr-only content correctly, with or without `clip-path` — the real gap exists but has no confirmed live visual effect, matching round 44/103's "correctly declined, no confirmed caller" precedent. Declined, not fixed.
- **Investigating `.visually-hidden`'s real host (MDN's own `<mdn-sidebar-filter>` search widget, a declarative-shadow-DOM custom element) surfaced something else entirely**: `getComputedStyle(el).display` reported `"none"` and `getBoundingClientRect()` was all-zero — yet the element carries NO CSS targeting it by name anywhere in the fetched stylesheets, and no `hidden` attribute. Reproduced from scratch with a minimal repro (`<my-widget><template shadowrootmode="open"><span>shadow content</span></template></my-widget>`, zero CSS at all) — `display:none`/all-zero rect regardless.
- **Root-caused to `layout.BuildIndex`'s own inline-item-to-element attribution walk (`layout/index.go`)**: a plain `display:inline` element "contributes no box of its own" (an existing, correct, long-standing design — see `appendElementInline`'s own "plain inline element" default case) and relies ENTIRELY on this walk unioning its descendants' inline-fragment rects up through `elementParent` (the light-DOM `.Parent` chain) to ever get a rect entry at all. But a shadow tree's top-level content has `Parent == nil` by design (`attachDeclarativeShadowRoots` marks it "the root of a separate tree") — so the walk dead-ends at the shadow content and NEVER crosses back to the host, however deeply it recurses. A shadow host that happens to be `display:block`/`inline-block` is unaffected (it gets its rect directly from its OWN Box, never needing this walk) — confirmed directly: both variants, tested in isolation, already worked correctly before this fix, isolating the bug precisely to the plain-inline case.
- **Fixed by teaching `elementParent` to bridge the gap it already knows about**: when a node's `Parent` is nil but its `ShadowHost` is set (the exact shape `attachDeclarativeShadowRoots` produces for shadow-tree-root content), return the host instead of stopping — the walk then continues up the HOST's own real light-DOM parent chain exactly as before. Scoped to `layout.elementParent` only (a separate, same-named-but-unrelated function from `js.elementParent`, which backs the REAL `.parentElement` DOM property and must keep returning `null` for shadow-tree-root content per spec — confirmed these are independent functions in independent packages before touching either).
- **Three new tests**: `layout.TestElementParentBridgesShadowHost` (the bridge itself, plus a genuinely detached non-shadow node still resolving to nil), `layout.TestBuildIndexShadowHostWithNoOwnBox` (the full attribution walk, using the same `Box`/`InlineItem` construction style as the existing `TestBuildIndexBlockAndInline`), and `engine.TestShadowHostGetComputedStyleAndRect` (full end-to-end through real `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` JS calls). Git-stash-confirmed: all three fail with the exact predicted symptom when the fix is reverted (`elementParent(shadow top-level node) = <nil>, want host`; a zero rect; `RESULT:display=none`). All five coverage floors held — dom/layout/paint/paginate unaffected, `layout` stays at 100.0%.
- **Honestly, this does NOT visibly change developer.mozilla.org's own `<mdn-sidebar-filter>` widget** — checked directly, not assumed: its entire containing `<aside class="layout__left-sidebar">` is correctly, deliberately hidden at this session's own 1024px bench viewport by a real, ALREADY-VERIFIED-CORRECT (round 103) `calc()`-based Media Queries Level 4 range query (`width < calc(...)` evaluating to a ~1072px breakpoint) — real Chrome hides it too at this exact width. The fix is a genuine `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` API-correctness bug for ANY plain-inline shadow host a script queries for layout decisions, confirmed via a from-scratch minimal repro independent of this specific page, but its value here is correctness, not a visible symptom on this corpus's current fetched content — the same honest pattern as round 50's `customElements` stub, round 52's `document.implementation`, and round 54's `TreeWalker`.

No page moved attributably — consistent with a geometry/JS-API correctness fix whose one confirmed real-world trigger (MDN's `<mdn-sidebar-filter>`) sits behind an unrelated, correctly-hidden responsive breakpoint at this corpus's viewport. ## 2026-09-26 (round 113) — `<template>.content` was entirely unimplemented (`undefined`, not a real inert DocumentFragment), silently breaking lit-html's own template-cloning path — this CLOSES round 112's own explicitly-left-open mystery: caniuse.com's "Current version | Dev version" toggle now renders (engine#206)

No fresh issue filed; main exactly in sync with round 112's merge (`f64be78`). A fresh full-corpus `Engine.JSLog` sweep found only already-documented, already-declined hits (react.dev's own long-characterised hydration/reskin crash, round 82; caniuse.com's own third-party `adsbygoogle.js` error) — no new lead there. Picked up round 112's own explicitly-flagged open mystery directly instead: the "Current version"/"Dev version" toggle genuinely never gets created anywhere in webengine's real DOM, even after round 112's DocumentFragment-unwrap fix.

- **Traced caniuse.com's real, fetched `js/bundle.js`** (not guessed from the minified symptom) to the exact IIFE that builds the toggle: `lit-html`'s own `render(html\`...\`, container)` call, where `container` comes from a helper this session's `js.newTreeWalker` doc comment (round 54/69) had ALREADY documented lit-html reading as `this.el.content` — a real, previously-noted, but never-followed-up usage of `HTMLTemplateElement.content`.
- **Confirmed via a minimal, isolated repro, not a guess**: `document.createElement('template')` then `'content' in tpl` → `false`; `tpl.innerHTML = '<b>x</b>'` places the `<b>` directly as the TEMPLATE's own light-DOM child (`tpl.children.length === 1`) rather than into an inert content fragment; `tpl.content` is `undefined`, and any real cloning code touching `.content.cloneNode(true)` throws `TypeError: Cannot read property 'cloneNode' of undefined`. This is a genuine, foundational HTML5 spec gap (`HTMLTemplateElement.content`), not specific to caniuse.com or lit-html.
- **Root-caused why this had never surfaced as a visible crash before**: no confirmed prior caller reached for `.content` at all — round 54/69's own TreeWalker fix made `document.createTreeWalker(...).nextNode()` work well enough for lit-html's PART-discovery pass to proceed without erroring, but never supplied `.content` itself, so any code path actually cloning a template's real markup silently produced nothing instead of throwing (this specific caniuse.com widget's own render call never appeared as a JSLog error at all — confirmed directly by instrumenting `Node.prototype.insertBefore`/`appendChild`/`document.createDocumentFragment` on the live page before this fix: the widget's own container fragment was created but NOTHING matching its content was ever inserted anywhere, consistent with a silently-empty clone rather than a thrown, logged error).
- **Fixed with a real `Content *Node` field on `dom.Node`**, set on every `<template>` however it originates (`dom.NewElement` for `document.createElement('template')`; `convertChildren` for one parsed from real HTML source — the ONE other place a template can come from, both updated together so they can't drift apart) to an inert `"#fragment"`-tagged node (reusing round 112's own fragment representation and its already-correct `AppendChild`/`InsertBefore` unwrap-on-insert handling, rather than inventing a second one). `SetInnerHTML`/`InnerHTML`/`serialize` (`dom/mutate.go`) all route through a new `htmlContainer` helper that redirects to `.Content` for a `<template>`, everything else unchanged. `attachDeclarativeShadowRoots` (`dom/shadow.go`) — whose OWN `ShadowRoot.Children` doc comment already said "the host's declarative `<template>`'s `.content`, hoisted out of the light DOM," describing the CORRECT model years before `.content` existed to back it — now genuinely hoists from `tmpl.Content.Children` instead of `tmpl.Children` directly. JS binding: a `content` accessor on `js/dom.go`'s element wrapper, gated to the `template` tag only, matching real spec (no other element has this property).
- **Six new tests** across all three layers: `dom.TestTemplateContentFromParse` (real HTML source), `dom.TestNewElementTemplateHasContent`/`TestSetInnerHTMLOnTemplateWritesContent` (JS-creation path + innerHTML round trip), `dom.TestTemplateWithoutShadowRootModeStaysPlainTemplate` (an EXISTING test corrected, not just extended — its original assertion that a plain template's content sat directly in `tmpl.Children` quietly encoded the old, spec-incomplete model; updated to check both `tmpl.Children` (now empty) and `tmpl.Content.Children`, matching this session's own round-70/111 precedent for a test that pinned an old bug as its expectation), and `js.TestTemplateContent` (the exact `.content.cloneNode(true)` pattern lit-html uses). Git-stash-confirmed: with the fix reverted, the `dom` package fails to even COMPILE (`tmpl.Content undefined`, an even stronger signal than a runtime failure) and the `js` test fails at runtime with the exact predicted symptom (`contentIn=false` then the predicted `TypeError`). All five coverage floors held — dom's own floor improved again (98.2%→98.4%).
- **Verified live against the REAL, currently-fetched caniuse.com page, end to end**: before this fix, `document.querySelector('.home__browser-score-options')` matched nothing and the toggle's own text was confirmed genuinely absent from the real DOM (round 112's own corrected, non-self-referential probe). After this fix, the SAME live probe finds `.home__browser-score-options` present with its real two `<button>`s ("Current version"/"Dev version"), and a corrected substring search (built from `['Cur','rent',...].join('')`, avoiding round 112's own self-match pitfall) confirms the text is now genuinely present in `document.body.innerHTML`. **This closes round 112's own explicitly-flagged open mystery** — the DocumentFragment-unwrap fix (round 112) and the `.content` fix (this round) turned out to be two INDEPENDENT bugs on the SAME lit-html render path, matching this session's own established "two bugs gating one symptom" pattern (rounds 55/56, 110/111), except both landed as separately-investigated, separately-shipped rounds rather than deliberately held back together.

### Bench (round 113)

**Honestly, caniuse.com's own SSIM does NOT show an improvement** — checked directly, not assumed: 0.663→0.659 (pixdiff flat at 18.1%), a small DIP despite a directly-confirmed, real, live fix. Page height correctly grew by 3px (1737px→1740px), exactly matching the newly-rendered toggle row now taking up real vertical space it previously didn't — and that same height change is itself evidence the fix is doing something real, not nothing. The SSIM dip is consistent with this session's own long-established "real fix, invisible-or-negative at full-page SSIM" pattern (rounds 51/52/54/56/60/67/106): a small, genuinely-improved region can still move the whole-page aggregate score the wrong way once every pixel below it shifts down by 3px and lands very slightly out of alignment with Chrome's own reference crop, which SSIM has no way to distinguish from "wrong." Direct DOM inspection (before: `.home__browser-score-options` matches nothing; after: matches the real two-button toggle) is the authoritative confirmation here, not this number. Every other page flat/noise-level, consistent with a fix scoped to one specific widget on one specific page.

## 2026-09-26 (round 112) — `appendChild`/`insertBefore` never unwrapped a DocumentFragment: it was inserted as a literal `<#fragment>` element instead of its own children becoming direct children of the target, breaking any CSS relying on that relationship — confirmed live on caniuse.com's own "Browser scores" widget, though the specific visible symptom that motivated the investigation remains separately unresolved (engine#205)

No fresh issue filed; main exactly in sync with round 111's merge (`e745532`). JSLog sweep byte-identical (exhausted since round 73). Continued round 111's own flagged, unexplained caniuse.com bench movement (+0.0229) as this round's own investigation starting point.

- **Diff-band detection on caniuse.com's own montage found a real, structural difference this time** (round 111's own top-of-page crop hadn't caught it): the "Browser scores" panel's "Current version | Dev version" toggle row, present in Chrome's render, entirely missing from webengine's — shifting every score bar below it up by one row.
- **A live `<script>` probe on the real page found something genuinely unusual**: `document.querySelector('.home__section--browser-scores').innerHTML` contained a literal `<#fragment></#fragment>` element sitting empty between the section's heading and its score list — `#fragment` is this engine's own internal tag for a `DocumentFragment` (see `document.createDocumentFragment`'s JS binding), which per spec should NEVER appear as a node in a rendered tree at all: appending a fragment to a real parent must move the fragment's OWN children into that position, leaving the (now-empty) fragment itself detached.
- **Confirmed with a minimal, from-scratch repro, bypassing the real page's own complexity entirely**: `frag.appendChild(a); frag.appendChild(b); target.appendChild(frag)` produced `target.innerHTML === "<#fragment><span>AAA</span><span>BBB</span></#fragment>"` — the fragment inserted as a literal wrapper element instead of being unwrapped, exactly matching the real page's own broken shape.
- **Root-caused to `dom.AppendChild`/`dom.InsertBefore`**, the two Go-level primitives EVERY JS-facing DOM mutation method (`appendChild`, `append`, `prepend`, `replaceChild`, `insertBefore`, `before`, `after`) already routes through — confirmed via a direct grep of every call site in `js/dom.go`. Neither primitive special-cased a fragment child at all, treating it exactly like any other element.
- **Fixed at this single choke point** (mutualiser, not four separate patches): both primitives now detect a fragment child (`Type==Element && Tag=="#fragment"`) and, instead of inserting it, recursively insert each of ITS OWN children at the same position, in order, leaving the fragment empty and still detached afterward — matching spec exactly, and automatically correct for every one of the seven JS-facing methods at once since they all funnel through these two functions.
- **Two new tests** (`dom/mutate_test.go`), covering both `AppendChild` and `InsertBefore`, confirming children are correctly reparented, order is preserved relative to an existing sibling/ref, and the fragment itself ends up empty and parentless. Git-stash-confirmed to fail with the exact predicted symptom (`[#fragment]` instead of `[a b]` in the resulting child list). All five coverage floors held — dom's own floor actually improved (98.1%→98.2%).
- **Honestly, this does NOT explain caniuse.com's own specific visible symptom** — checked directly, not assumed: even with the fragment fix applied, the "Current version"/"Dev version" toggle text is STILL genuinely absent from webengine's real rendered DOM. A first check appeared to show the text existing elsewhere in `document.body.innerHTML`, but that was a methodology bug in the probe itself — the injected diagnostic `<script>`'s own source code literally contained the search string, matching itself; a corrected probe (building the search string from `['Cur','rent',...].join('')` so it never appears as one contiguous substring in the script's own serialized source) confirms the text is genuinely nowhere in the real DOM at all. The fragment bug is real, confirmed, and fixed regardless of not explaining this specific case; the toggle's own absence remains a separate, not-yet-root-caused mystery — correctly left open rather than falsely claimed as resolved.

### Bench (round 112)

No corpus page shows a movement clearly attributable to this fix — consistent with a real DOM-correctness fix confirmed via targeted unit tests and a controlled repro rather than this corpus's own aggregate SSIM, the same pattern as round 50's `customElements` stub, round 52's `document.implementation`, and round 54's `TreeWalker` (all real, confirmed fixes whose value shows in crash-prevention/correctness, not necessarily a bench number). caniuse.com's own SSIM is back to flat (+0.0019 vs the committed baseline) on this fresh fetch, confirming round 111's own flagged +0.0229 movement genuinely was live-content noise as suspected, not anything code-related. news.ycombinator.com's -0.0293 is its own well-documented front-page churn. Every other page flat or noise-level.

## 2026-09-26 (round 111) — the flex shrink phase had no "automatic minimum size" floor, so a `min-width:auto` item could be squeezed straight through zero — round 110's own precise, ready-to-ship follow-up, now shipped (engine#204)

No fresh issue filed; main exactly in sync with round 110's merge (`26e297a`). JSLog sweep byte-identical (exhausted since round 73). This round ships the exact, already-coded, already-validated follow-up round 110 deliberately held back — CSS's "automatic minimum size": a flex item whose `min-width` is `auto` (the default) must never shrink below its own min-content width, unless its overflow is non-visible in that axis (spec's own carve-out).

- **Restored the stashed patch from round 110's own investigation** (`flexItem.minContent`, computed via `minContentWidth`; `resolveMainRow`'s shrink clamp raises its floor to `minContent` when `MinWidth.Auto && OverflowX==Visible`) and added three new tests. Two of the three passed immediately; the third — reusing `minContentWidth` directly for `minContent` — broke TWO pre-existing tests (`TestFlexShrink`, `TestFlexShrinkCascadesPastAFrozenItem`).
- **Root-caused the regression rather than loosening the new tests to match**: `minContentWidth`'s own "a definite width is the contribution outright" branch is correct for its EXISTING caller (a table column's width floor, where an author's declared cell width should act as a hard minimum) but WRONG for a flex item's automatic minimum size, which must reflect the item's own CONTENT regardless of any width the author declared — shrinking a definite-width item below its declared width is the entire point of flex-shrink. Fixed by measuring `minContent` with the item's own `Width` forced back to `css.Length{Auto:true}` before calling `minContentWidth` — the IDENTICAL pattern `layout/table.go`'s own column-minimum computation already uses, for the identical reason (its own doc comment: "measured with the width treated as auto and the declaration applied afterwards").
- **`TestFlexShrinkCascadesPastAFrozenItem` needed its own expectations updated, not just its code path**: its ORIGINAL assertions (`A.W=0`) encoded the OLD, less-spec-compliant "floor at 0 with nowhere left to shrink" assumption for an item whose real content ("A", one unbreakable character) genuinely has a 10px automatic minimum — real Chrome would also stop shrinking it there. Updated to the correct, hand-verified values (`A.W=10, B.W=190, B.X=10`) with an expanded doc comment explaining why, matching this session's own round-70 precedent for a pre-existing test that had quietly encoded an old bug as its own expectation.
- **All three new tests plus both corrected pre-existing ones pass**; git-stash-confirmed to fail with the exact predicted symptoms without the fix. All five coverage floors held.
- **Verified against the REAL, live github.com/golang/go page**: "Pricing" is back to its full, natural 70.08px width (no longer even partially squeezed, unlike round 110's own intermediate 5.4px result before this follow-up).

### Bench (round 111)

github.com/golang/go itself barely moves (+0.0005), matching round 110's own established expectation (the nav strip is a small fraction of a 2528px page). caniuse.com moved by a real, notable +0.0229 (pixdiff 17.8%→16.0%) — checked directly, not assumed: cropped the page's own top region (nav + search bar) and found no visible difference attributable to this round's specific fix; the page's own headline date banner matches exactly between fetches (ruling out the most obvious content-refresh explanation), but no clear single visual cause was found within this round's investigation budget. Reported honestly as unexplained-but-plausible live-content variance on an actively-maintained, frequently-updated site, not attributed to this fix without evidence. Every other page flat or noise-level (pkg.go.dev +0.0036, go.dev/blog +0.0007); news.ycombinator.com's -0.0229 is its own well-documented front-page churn.

## Older history

Rounds up to and including round 110 (everything from round 109 down through
the original Phase 1/2 build-out — roughly the project's first ~145 logged
rounds) are archived in
[FIDELITY-ARCHIVE.md](FIDELITY-ARCHIVE.md), split out on 2026-11-09 purely
to keep this file under gitsafe's 1 MiB per-commit blob push guard as it
kept growing. Nothing was rewritten, trimmed or summarized — only moved,
byte for byte. A round mentioned below by number (e.g. "round 105", "round
77") that isn't above this note lives there.

## Known gaps (updated 2026-08-30 — see the note above on why this drifted)

- **PARTIALLY CLOSED for `WebAssembly.compileStreaming`/`instantiateStreaming` and the module-size/linear-memory caps (2026-11-03, engine#341) — see round 189's own entry just below, now updated: both ARE implemented, and both caps are now configurable (`Engine.MaxWasmModuleBytes`/`MaxWasmMemoryBytes`) rather than hardcoded. Everything else round 189 already disclosed as unimplemented (the synchronous `Module`/`Instance` constructors, Table/Global imports/exports, standalone `Memory`/`Table` objects, WASI) remains exactly as disclosed there — this round did not touch any of them.** `compileStreaming`/`instantiateStreaming` are not genuine incremental streaming (this engine's `fetch()` has no streaming body to begin with — see the round's own log entry above), but the JS-facing Promise/Response contract is satisfied exactly, which is what every real caller's own code actually observes.
- **CLOSED for `window.postMessage` (round 201, 2026-11-02, engine#340) — a no-op; real cross-window message delivery is not simulated (this engine's single-document model has no second window/frame to deliver to; `MessagePort`'s own `postMessage`, via `MessageChannel`, already delivers for real and is unaffected).** Did not exist as a global at all before this round — only `MessagePort.postMessage` did. Confirmed real trigger: Google's own widely-deployed reCAPTCHA script (`gstatic.com/recaptcha/...`), which calls it unconditionally at load; reproduced live on spotify.com as `ReferenceError: postMessage is not defined`.
- **CLOSED for `document.referrer` (round 200, 2026-11-01, engine#339) — always returns the empty string (this engine's single-URL fetch model has no referring-page history to report).** Did not exist at all before this round. Confirmed real trigger: Fathom Analytics' own widely-served `script.js`, whose `trackPageview()` calls `document.referrer.indexOf(hostname)` unconditionally — identically on vuejs.org and astro.build, two otherwise-unrelated sites sharing the same third-party script.
- **`<canvas>.getContext(...)` is entirely unimplemented — confirmed real trigger: stripe.com's own bundle calls it repeatedly (round 200, 2026-11-01), `TypeError: Object has no member 'getContext'`.** Re-investigated round 201 (2026-11-02), still declined: `grep -rn "getContext|CanvasRenderingContext|canvas" js/` finds zero rasterization machinery anywhere in this engine to build a narrow stub on (unlike `clip-path`, round 194, which reused existing mask/group-buffer paint infrastructure) — a real `CanvasRenderingContext2D` surface, even a minimal inert one, needs genuine 2D rasterization semantics from scratch. The real-world usage (a "squeezy-carousel" effect name) suggests per-frame pixel manipulation (`drawImage`/`getImageData`-shaped), not a write-only case a cheap no-op could satisfy. **A separate puzzle surfaced, not yet explained**: rendering stripe.com through this engine's own pipeline with `JSLog` capture produced ZERO log lines at all — none of its 85 `<script>` tags executed, despite the scripts themselves being independently confirmed fetchable — blocking confirmation of the actual canvas call pattern. Worth its own dedicated look before any future attempt at this gap.
- **CLOSED for `document.location` (round 199, 2026-11-01, engine#338) — it did not exist at all before this round, only `window.location`.** `js/window.go` set `location` on the global object only; fixed by building the one `Location` object once and setting it on both `window` and `document` (they must be the SAME object per the HTML Standard — calling the constructor twice would let the two diverge after either's `assign()`/`replace()`). Confirmed real trigger: Cloudflare's own web-analytics beacon script, served on an enormous share of real-world sites, reads `document.location.href` directly.
- **`import(...)` (dynamic import, in ANY script, not only `<script type="module">`) is rejected by goja's parser as a reserved word — confirmed directly against the pinned version (round 199, 2026-11-01).** DIFFERENT from the already-documented `for await` gap (a different ES2018+ construct) and from the already-solved static-`import`/`export`-in-module-scripts case (`modules.go`'s esbuild bundling, which only applies to `<script type="module">` and only helps because those imports are statically resolvable at build time). A dynamic `import()` with a runtime-computed argument — Astro's own universal client-hydration mechanism (`<astro-island>`'s bootstrap script, a classic, non-module `<script>`), and likely other frameworks' lazy-loading patterns — cannot be bundled away the same way, since the whole point is deferring resolution past build time. Confirmed this is the actual reason `<astro-island>` elements never hydrate on any Astro site in this engine: the custom element's own definition never compiles, so `customElements.define("astro-island", ...)` never runs — a more fundamental blocker than the IntersectionObserver-stub theory rounds 195/196 raised for the same symptom, which is never even reached. No JS parser lives in this repo to patch; not fixable here.
- **CLOSED for `<picture>`/`<source>`/`srcset` (round 195, 2026-11-01, engine#334) — a `<source>`'s `type` attribute and `srcset` width/density descriptors (picking the best of several candidates) remain unimplemented; only the first comma-separated candidate of the first `media`-matching `<source>` is ever taken.** Before this round, `srcset` did not exist anywhere in the Go source — every `<img>` was read by its own bare `src`, with zero awareness a `<picture>` ancestor could override it. Confirmed real trigger: en.wikipedia.org's own footer logos, unconditionally present in static markup on every article page, whose single `<source>` per `<picture>` has one type-less, descriptor-less `srcset` each — exactly what the narrower implementation covers.
- **CLOSED for `clip-path: polygon()` (round 194, 2026-10-31, engine#330), including a `calc()` term mixing a percentage with an absolute length — a `<geometry-box>` keyword (border-box is always used), `circle()`/`ellipse()`/`inset()`/`path()`, `round <length>` vertex rounding, a `url()` reference to an SVG `<clipPath>` element, and clip-path on a `::before`/`::after` pseudo-element remain unimplemented.** Investigated and correctly declined at least three times before this round (every prior occurrence found was the invisible `sr-only` idiom or an unrelated SVG attribute false positive) — vercel.com's own homepage is the first confirmed VISIBLE trigger: a decorative "paper-peel" corner cut, `clip-path:polygon(0 0, 100% 0, 100% calc(100% - 112.00px), calc(100% - 112.00px) 100%, 0 100%)`, directly in static markup. The polygon's hard edge has no antialiasing (the same simplification the legacy `clip:rect()` path already makes).
- **CLOSED for `HTMLFormElement.requestSubmit()`/`.submit()` (round 192, 2026-10-31, engine#328) — per-control constraint validation (checking every control and firing "invalid" on failures, which `requestSubmit()` is specified to run first) and an actual network submission/page navigation when the event is not cancelled remain unimplemented.** Before this round, `form.requestSubmit()` did not exist at all. Confirmed real trigger: linear.app's own fetched bundle calls it directly. No confirmed trigger needs form-level validation; no actual navigation is possible from this engine's single-document settle regardless — the same disclosed limitation `window.location.assign()`/`.reload()` already have.
- **CSS Grid Level 2 `subgrid` (`grid-template-columns:subgrid`/`grid-template-rows:subgrid`) is unimplemented — investigated round 191 (2026-10-30) and correctly declined, not a narrow parser addition.** Confirmed real trigger: stripe.com/pricing's own inline stylesheet sets `grid-template-columns:subgrid` four times (e.g. `.ProductsCallout--desktop{grid-column:1/span 4;display:grid;grid-template-columns:subgrid;...}`), on elements that are themselves grid items meant to align their own children to the PARENT grid's column tracks. `css/grid.go`'s `parseTrackList` already drops the whole declaration on the literal token `subgrid` (its own doc comment already named this), but recognising the token is not the real blocker: `layout/grid.go`'s `grid()` lays out each item via `l.layoutIsolated(it.node, it.st, spanW-...)` (line 72, read directly before concluding this) — a single scalar width, with NO path for the parent's own already-computed per-track `colW`/`colX` arrays (`sizeColumns`, line 66) to reach a subgrid child's OWN subsequent `grid()` call, which would otherwise size its columns independently from its own (absent) template. Real support needs a new data-flow path — threading parent track geometry into a child's layout call — a materially larger change than this round's own budget, not a few-line fix; revisit if a narrower sub-case (e.g. a child spanning the SAME column count with no own line names to resolve) turns out tractable on a closer read.
- **CLOSED for `font-size: clamp()` with `rem`/`vw`/`px`/`em`/`%` terms, no percentage mixed with `vw` in the SAME term (round 190, 2026-10-30, engine#326) — `clamp()` on any OTHER property, and `min()`/`max()` anywhere, remain unimplemented.** Found live on carbondesignsystem.com (`font-size:clamp(4rem,13vw,12rem)`, fluid heading sizes). Also fixed, found along the way rather than assumed: a bare `font-size:13vw` (no `clamp()` at all) was ALREADY silently resolving against the wrong basis — the parent's font-size instead of the viewport width — before this round; see `css.resolveFontSizeLength`'s own doc comment for why font-size's EAGER (cascade-time, not layout-time) percentage/vw resolution is what makes this tractable where the identical-looking `min()`/`max()` gap on width-like properties genuinely is not.
- **CLOSED for `WebAssembly.compile`/`instantiate`/`validate`, a module's own exported functions and linear memory (round 189, 2026-10-30, engine#325) — the synchronous `Module`/`Instance` constructors, Table/Global imports and exports, standalone `Memory`/`Table` objects, and WASI (`wasi_snapshot_preview1`) remain unimplemented.** Before this round `WebAssembly` did not exist as a global at all. No confirmed page/container has needed any of the remaining pieces yet; a module assuming a WASI host (stdio, filesystem, clocks) fails to instantiate with a LinkError naming the exact missing import, an honest outcome rather than a silent wrong answer. (`compileStreaming`/`instantiateStreaming` and configurable module/memory caps, also once listed here as unimplemented, were closed 2026-11-03 — see the entry just above.)
- **CLOSED for `oklab()`/`oklch()` (round 182, 2026-10-26, engine#318) — `lab()`/`lch()` (CIE Lab, a separate conversion) and the `none` component keyword remain unrecognised.** `oklab()`/`oklch()` as a plain colour value (not only inside `color-mix()`, which already had the conversion math) were genuinely unimplemented, found live on w3.org's own CSS Color 4 spec page. `lab()`/`lch()` need their own, not-yet-written sRGB conversion (CIE Lab's D50 white point and non-linear transfer differ from OKLab's), and `none` has no confirmed trigger — both left for a future round if one appears.
- **No real CSS-transition/animation-timeline simulation — a page whose own JavaScript drives a `setInterval`-based crossfade (toggling `element.style.opacity` on a delay, relying on a `transition:opacity` declaration to animate smoothly between states) renders whatever opacity values script execution happens to leave behind when this engine's settle loop stops, not a single coherent frame of a real timeline.** Found round 145 (2026-09-28), on — of all pages — `example.com`, which gained real client-side JS for the first time in this session's whole history: a newly-added script cross-fades through six `<p lang="…">` translations stacked in the same CSS Grid cell via per-character `<span>` opacity toggles on a 5-second `setInterval`, each with its own `transitionDelay`. Several languages' spans render simultaneously above zero opacity, overlapping into illegible layered text — confirmed via git-stash to reproduce identically on pre-round-145 code, so this is not new breakage, only newly exposed by a page that had never needed animation-timeline behaviour before. Not fixed: real support needs an actual model of elapsed time, `transition` interpolation, and `setInterval` callback scheduling relative to it — a materially larger feature than any of this session's own transform/layout rounds, and no other confirmed corpus trigger has needed it yet.
- **Multi-column layout (`columns`/`column-count`/`column-width`) models only what pkg.go.dev's own confirmed trigger needs — N even columns, each in-flow element child placed atomically by a single greedy height-bucketing pass — not the full spec (round 144, 2026-09-28, engine#244; `layout/multicol.go`).** Round 143 found the gap real but judged a full implementation too large to attempt alongside that round's own `word-break` fix; round 144 re-examined that judgment bibliography-first (reading the actual spec mechanics) and found the ONE confirmed trigger — a `<ul>` of short, uniform-height `<li>`s — genuinely narrower than arbitrary flowing prose. What's still NOT modelled, precisely because nothing in this engine's own corpus has triggered a need for it: `column-span:all` (one child spanning every column), `column-rule` (the decorative divider line between columns — the plain gap alone is already the confirmed visual difference from a single column), true iterative column-height balancing (this engine's single greedy pass approximates it closely for a roughly-uniform list, but can leave a visibly taller last column for very unevenly-sized content), and fragmenting a single child's own internal content — its own wrapped text lines, or its own nested children — across two columns (a real browser's balancing can split one long paragraph's lines between columns; this engine treats every child as one atomic, unsplittable unit).
- **react.dev's own hydration fails with a real React error (#418, "the initial UI does not match what was rendered on the server"), which cascades into a SECOND, internal React crash during its own unmount/remount recovery and unmounts the whole app to nothing — this engine's own empty-page guard then falls back to the pre-hydration SSR skeleton, the long-documented "reskin gap."** Found live (round 82, 2026-09-22): this is a NEW, more precisely characterised failure mode, distinct from round 71's `ChunkLoadError` theory — confirmed unrelated by fixing round 71's own flagged root cause (`setTimeout` discarding its requested delay, fixed this same round) and re-checking: the live JS log was byte-for-byte identical before and after that fix. One plausible theory (a `prefers-color-scheme` client/server mismatch via `matchMedia`) was checked and ruled out — this engine already deliberately resolves that query to match react.dev's own dark-theme strategy. Minified production React strips the hydration-mismatch diff that would name the exact divergent DOM node, so finding the actual cause needs a materially larger investigation than fits in one round; not chased further, not fixed here.
- **goja (this engine's JS runtime) does not support ES2018 `for await (... of ...)` asynchronous-iteration syntax.** Found live on tailwindcss.com (round 77, 2026-09-21): a real Next.js chunk shipped as a plain classic script (correctly recognised as such by this engine — no type-attribute mishandling involved) fails to compile at all because its own code legitimately uses `for await` inside a real `async function`. Isolated to the exact byte via the real fetched chunk, and reproduced from scratch with a minimal, from-nothing repro containing only the `for await` construct. Confirmed as a genuine, currently-open upstream gap (dop251/goja#498, "Upgrade to latest version of ECMAScript 2021/2022") rather than a local misconfiguration — there is no JS parser in this repo to patch; forking or patching goja itself is outside this engine's own scope. Not fixed, not fixable here.
- **`display:inline-grid`/`inline-table` — re-checked round 148 (2026-10-04): the only corpus use of `inline-grid` is tailwindcss.com's header search button and theme switcher, both DIRECT CHILDREN of a `display:flex` container, where a flex item is blockified regardless — so the missing inline half has no visible effect on any corpus page. No confirmed trigger yet; revisit if one appears.**
- **Historical, now CLOSED: `display:inline-flex` once lost its "inline" half.** `DisplayInlineFlex` now exists and `layout.go`'s `appendElementInline` lays it out as an atomic inline box with a `NestedBox` (verified round 148, 2026-10-04). The original report, kept for context: confirmed on TWO separate real pages for `inline-flex`:
  github.com's header "Sign in"/"Sign up" buttons (round 24) and pkg.go.dev's
  breadcrumb `<li>`s (round 26, `.go-Breadcrumb li{display:inline-flex}`),
  where each list item stacks on its own line instead of flowing in a row
  with `>` separators between them (the separators were ALSO missing at the
  time — a second gap, rank 5 in the original Phase-0 fidelity plan, now
  CLOSED for the literal-string case; see the dedicated entry below).
  This was once filed as "the SAME architectural gap as the
  `<react-partial>`-flattens-block-content one, from the opposite
  direction" and "not attempted" — both stale by the time this correction
  was made (2026-10-18): round 38 (engine#118, 2026-09-05) built exactly
  the "atomic inline-level box with a real box/layout inside" mechanism
  this bullet said the engine "has never had," and round 148 wired
  `inline-flex` specifically through it. Left as a historical record of
  how long a cross-referenced gap can keep citing a dependency that
  closed weeks earlier without anyone reconciling the citation — see the
  `<react-partial>` entry below, now also marked CLOSED.
- **CLOSED for the confirmed real trigger (round 177, 2026-10-21, engine#303) — `::before`/`::after` generated content, a `content:"<literal>"` string only.** Cited as "comparable in scope to Shadow DOM's own multi-round build-out" across rounds 44/56/57/87 (MDN's breadcrumb separators and theme-toggle icon, Wikipedia's edit-section brackets, pkg.go.dev's own breadcrumb) — closed with a narrower design than any of those write-ups assumed: a new `content`/`ContentSet` pair on `css.Style`, a `Selector.MatchesPseudoElement` entry point (ordinary `Matches` still correctly fails every pseudo-element compound, including ::before/::after reached through it — only this NEW entry point can match one), `computePseudoElement` (cascade.go) resolving a ::before/::after rule's own declarations with the real element as its inheritance parent, and layout's `renderedChildren` — already the one documented substitution point every shadow-aware walk goes through — synthesising a leading/trailing element for `Before`/`After` when set. A page with no stylesheet targeting either pseudo-element takes the exact same code path as before this existed, at every one of those layers. Verified live end-to-end (not just unit tests): a breadcrumb fixture renders "Home > Docs > Page" with the correct colour on the generated separators. **Deliberately narrower than full CSS generated content**: only a plain quoted-string `content` value is understood (`attr()`/`counter()`/`url()`/`open-quote` drop the whole declaration, same as any other unsupported value in this codebase); whitespace inside the content string collapses the same way ordinary source text does (`appendWords` does not distinguish the two), where a real browser preserves it literally. **The `background-image`/`mask-image` half closed separately (round 181, 2026-10-26, engine#317) — see the dedicated entry below.** A form control's own `::before`/`::after` is still blocked by it being laid out as a wholly atomic box (rounds 55-56's own disclosed limit) — this is why MDN's own hamburger-menu `<button>::before` and `.color-theme__option`/`.color-theme__button` theme-toggle `<button>::before` (both confirmed live, round 181, to be real `<button>` elements) stay invisible regardless of this round's fix. **Correcting a stale citation**: an earlier round's note on this gap described MDN's breadcrumb separator CSS as `content:">"` with a `mask-image` layered on top; fetching the page's current, live stylesheet (round 181) found the real rule is `content:""` (not `">"`) — the separator is ENTIRELY the mask-image icon, with no literal-text fallback. The `">"` description belongs to pkg.go.dev's own, textually-different breadcrumb pattern (round 177's trigger), not MDN's.
- **The third-party SVG rasteriser (`github.com/srwiley/oksvg`, pinned at its
  latest available commit) mis-renders at least one real-world complex
  multi-subpath vector `<path>`.** Found live on developer.mozilla.org
  (2026-09-03, round 25, no code change possible in THIS engine): its "mdn"
  logo wordmark, drawn as vector letterforms in a single `<path>` (a common
  technique for a short brand mark, avoiding a web-font dependency), renders
  as overlapping/doubled glyph shapes. Isolated with three independent
  reproductions, the last calling `oksvg.ReadIconStream`+`rasterx` directly
  with NO go-webengine code in the call path at all — conclusive proof this
  is not a bug in this engine's own SVG handling (`svg.go`'s path
  extraction/viewBox scaling/target sizing). The pinned commit is oksvg's
  newest available one, so there is no newer upstream fix to pick up by
  bumping the dependency. Fixing (or forking) a third-party rasteriser's own
  path-fill-rule/subpath handling is outside this engine's own codebase —
  not attempted.
- **The bundled "Go Mono" font's comma glyph loses its descending tail below
  14px, rendering as a period.** Found live on pkg.go.dev's own code blocks
  (round 64, 2026-09-07; `pre,textarea.code{font-family:SFMono-Regular,
  Consolas,Liberation Mono,Menlo,monospace;font-size:.875rem}` — none of the
  named faces are bundled, so it falls to the generic `monospace` keyword,
  this engine's Go Mono), where `resp, err := http.Get(...)` reads as
  `resp. err`. Isolated to an EXACT size threshold via a 10-24px sweep:
  10-14px render the comma with no visible tail at all, 15px and above render
  it correctly — and isolated to Go Mono specifically, not a general small-
  size rasterisation issue: the SAME 14px size renders proportional-font
  (Inter/Lora) commas correctly, and even Go Mono's OWN letter descenders
  (g/j/p/q/y) render with normal, clearly visible tails at 14px — only the
  comma glyph is affected. The font itself comes from a separate dependency
  (`github.com/go-opentype/fonts/gomono`, not vendored in this repo), and its
  rasterisation goes through `github.com/go-opentype/opentype`'s own
  `Face.render`/`rasterize` — hinting is confirmed OFF by default in this
  engine's own font setup (`paint/fonts.go` never calls `SetHinting`), ruling
  out a hinting misconfiguration on this engine's side. Whether the actual
  defect is in the font's own glyph outline or in the rasteriser's handling
  of a small, thin, curved shape was not determined — narrowing further
  requires instrumenting go-opentype's own rasteriser directly, outside this
  repo. Not fixed here: the font and its rasteriser are both a separate
  dependency, not this engine's own code.
- **[FIXED 2026-10-19 in `go-gfx/oksvg` v0.2.0 (arc flags without separators; see the entry at the top) — the next two bullets are kept as history.]**
- **The third-party SVG rasteriser (`github.com/srwiley/oksvg`) mis-renders a
  SECOND real-world complex `<path>`**, extending round 25's already-
  documented finding (the MDN wordmark) with a concrete new example: GitHub's
  own octocat logo (the exact `<path>` tailwindcss.com's header links to
  `github.com/tailwindlabs/tailwindcss` with) renders as a small, wrong-shaped
  white crescent instead of the full silhouette. Isolated with a minimal
  standalone repro — a bare `<svg viewBox="0 0 20 20"><path fill="white"
  d="...">` with the exact real path data, NO Tailwind classes, NO CSS, NO
  other markup at all — reproducing the identical broken shape, ruling out
  any involvement of this engine's own class/style handling (fill-opacity
  utilities, `serializeSVG`'s XML regeneration, viewBox scaling) and
  confirming the defect is purely inside oksvg's own path-fill rasterisation,
  matching round 25's own conclusion pattern exactly. Not fixed here, for the
  same reason: a third-party rasteriser's own path-handling is outside this
  engine's codebase.
- **A THIRD confirmed real-world example (round 79, 2026-09-21)**: en.wikipedia.org's
  own site wordmark and tagline logos (`wikipedia-wordmark-en-25.svg`,
  `wikipedia-tagline-en-25.svg` — the current 25th-anniversary variants,
  vector letterforms as complex `<path>` data, the same general shape as
  round 25's MDN wordmark) both render with garbled black shapes overlapping
  several letters (a bowtie/hourglass blob across "IK"/"PE" in "WIKIPEDIA";
  small solid dots in place of "y"/"c" in the tagline "25 years of the free
  encyclopedia"). Isolated with the SAME two-step methodology as round 64: an
  `<img src="data:image/svg+xml;base64,...">` repro with the real fetched SVG
  bytes and NO other page markup, then a standalone Go program calling
  `oksvg.ReadIconStream`/`rasterx.NewDasher` DIRECTLY with zero go-webengine
  code anywhere in the call path — both reproduce the identical garbled
  shapes for both logos. Not fixed, for the same reason as the two prior
  instances: this is oksvg's own path rasterisation, not this engine's.
- **An inline element's box decoration is painted (round 48) but not
  completely**: solid `background-color`, `border` and `padding` fragment per
  line box with `box-decoration-break: slice`, and `border-radius` is honoured
  on a complete fragment; `background-image`/gradients, `box-shadow`,
  `outline` and `filter` on an inline element are still unpainted (they live
  on the `*layout.Box` path only), `box-decoration-break: clone` is not
  parsed, and a fragment's content height is the item's LINE BOX height rather
  than the font's own em box — so a large `line-height` makes the band taller
  than a browser's.

- **CLOSED (round 38, 2026-09-05, engine#118) — a block/flex/grid-level element nested under an inline-context ancestor (e.g. an unstyled custom element, which defaults to `display:inline`) used to be flattened into plain inline text instead of getting a real box.** This bullet sat in "Known gaps" marked "not attempted" for six weeks after the fix actually shipped — caught 2026-10-18 while drafting a plan to implement it, only to find `InlineItem.BlockBreak`/`placeInlineSegments` (`layout/layout.go`) already there, with passing regression tests (`layout/blockinline_test.go`) confirmed live on current (v0.7.2) code. The original two confirmed triggers, kept for context:

  **github.com/golang/go** (2026-09-03, round 24): its whole marketing header sat inside `<react-partial>`, a custom element nothing styles — `appendElementInline` had no check for a nested element's OWN display resolving to block-level, so it unconditionally recursed via `appendInline`, losing box model/margins/flex layout for everything inside (two adjacent buttons rendered glued together with no gap).

  **news.ycombinator.com** (2026-09-04, round 33): its upvote-triangle icon, `<a href='vote?...'><div class='votearrow'></div></a>` — a plain block `<div>` (background-image icon, no text) nested directly inside an inline `<a>` — lost its box the same way, confirmed with an isolated, network-independent `RenderHTML` reproduction that ruled out the background-image pipeline itself as the cause.

  Round 38 fixed both by reading WebKit's and Gecko's own source for how each models block-in-inline content first (per this project's own bibliography-first rule), then implementing a flat `BlockBreak` sentinel suited to this engine's one-shot (no persistent render tree) architecture rather than porting either reference engine's own continuation/ib-split machinery: `appendElementInline` now checks a child's own resolved `display` and, when it is block-level, emits a `BlockBreak` sentinel instead of recursing into it as inline content; `placeInlineSegments` splits the surrounding inline-item run at each sentinel, wrapping each ordinary-item segment in its own anonymous block box and placing the promoted element as a real sibling via the normal block/flex/grid/table dispatch — CSS 2.1 §9.2.1.1, firing at any nesting depth under an inline ancestor. Confirmed live at the time: news.ycombinator.com's upvote arrow now paints (SSIM 0.564→0.579) and tailwindcss.com's syntax-highlighted code demo renders one line per row instead of squashed onto one (SSIM 0.666→0.704). github.com's own original trigger markup had since changed and could not be re-verified live; covered instead by a synthetic regression test built from the originally-documented shape.
- **No optional-ligature ("liga") or GPOS shaping (kerning, mark
  attachment) — only the mandatory "rlig" GSUB feature is applied (round
  141, engine#241).** This entry used to say this engine had no
  `@font-face` at all and that fixing Material Icons' invisible nav carets
  would need architecture "genuinely comparable in scope to Shadow DOM" —
  both premises are now stale: `@font-face` fetching/WOFF2 decoding shipped
  separately (engine#233/#234/#236), and `go-opentype/opentype`'s `Face`
  gained a `Shape`/`GlyphMaskIndex` API directly (no separate shaping
  package, as this entry previously assumed) that round 141 wired in for
  the one GSUB feature real usage confirmed a need for. What is genuinely
  still missing, narrower than before: `font-variant-ligatures`/`liga`
  (cosmetic ffi/fl-style ligatures, and their real-browser interaction with
  non-zero `letter-spacing` — not plumbed through `Measure`'s signature,
  see `requiredLigatureFeature`'s own doc comment) and GPOS-based
  positioning (`ShapePositioned` exists in the dependency but nothing calls
  it) — pair kerning and mark-to-base attachment (diacritics riding a base
  glyph) still don't happen. No confirmed real-world trigger for either has
  been found yet; revisit if one turns up.
- **Form controls now paint (2026-09-03, engine#95 — a general
  `input`/`button`/`select`/`textarea` atomic box + paint mechanism, not
  previously present at all) and `<select>`'s sizing/label followed up to be
  standards-correct (2026-09-03, engine#96, see the log entry below)**:
  remaining gaps are `<input>`'s value/placeholder text not being
  pixel-perfect UA chrome (close enough to read as intentional, not exact),
  and no actual popup/listbox surface for `<select>` (this engine has no
  click-driven interactive UI beyond a settle pass at all).
- **A checkbox/radio with `appearance:none` now honours its own cascaded
  background/background-image/border/border-radius instead of always taking
  this engine's generic square (2026-09-28, round 146, engine#246)** —
  confirmed load-bearing live: developer.mozilla.org's own `<mdn-switch>`
  toggle-switch component. Without `appearance:none` (the common case — an
  ordinary, unstyled checkbox) nothing changed: the generic square, with no
  real checkmark glyph and no distinction between checkbox/radio shape, is
  still what paints (round 138's own disclosed limit). Only the bare
  `none`/`auto` keywords are modelled for `appearance` itself — the CSS Basic
  UI vendor-specific keywords (`textfield`, `menulist-button`, `searchfield`,
  ...) have no confirmed trigger and are left unsupported.
- **The settle loop's empty-render guard (`reskin()`, engine#84) cannot
  restyle a node that a rejected script pass REMOVED from the DOM — and this
  is a BIGGER-BLAST-RADIUS gap than earlier rounds' own write-up suggested.**
  First confirmed live on react.dev (2026-09-02, engine#92) as affecting
  "the hero `<h1>` and one CTA button". Re-measured 2026-09-03 (round 19,
  no code change) by actually counting: after settle, react.dev's DOM has
  **ZERO `<h2>` elements at all** — the SAME router-failure script pass that
  toggles dark mode unmounts the ENTIRE marketing homepage's content
  sections ("Create user interfaces from components", "Write components
  with code and markup", every subsequent section), not just the hero. Every
  one of those nodes keeps its pre-failure (light-mode) style forever, on a
  now-dark background — the washed-out, low-contrast prose visible
  throughout the whole page below the very top fold is this SAME root
  cause, just far more of the page than previously documented. A general
  fix would need to diff the DOM before/after a rejected pass (to
  distinguish "removed and needs its last-known style" from "still present,
  re-cascade normally"), or re-cascade an orphaned subtree against a
  synthetic root reproducing its lost ancestor chain (its OWN `.Parent` is
  nulled by `dom.RemoveChild` at the moment of removal, so the chain isn't
  recoverable after the fact without having snapshotted it before) — still
  not attempted: a real architectural addition, not a narrow fix, and now a
  stronger candidate for a dedicated future session given the corrected,
  much larger measured scope.

Several bullets that stood here since Phase 0/1 were flatly wrong by the time of
the 2026-08-30 audit — JavaScript, external stylesheets, `overflow` clipping,
gradients/box-shadow/border-radius and real bold/italic fonts had all since
shipped (Phase 1.5–2.0, and the font work logged in the org's project memory).
Restated against what was actually verified that day:

- **Shadow DOM: CLOSED for the declarative case** (2026-08-31, engine#73/#75/
  #77 — see the log entry above for the full account). Declarative
  `<template shadowrootmode>` attachment, `<slot>` distribution (default and
  named, including correct non-rendering of unmatched/unslotted light
  content), and `:host`/`:host()` CSS scoping all ship and are verified
  against developer.mozilla.org's real markup (which uses exactly this
  mechanism — confirmed via raw fetch, 23 declarative shadow roots).
  **Imperative `attachShadow()`/`customElements.define()` CLOSED for the
  confirmed real trigger (round 183, 2026-10-26, engine#319)** — see the
  dedicated log entry; the "neither confirmed real site needed it" premise
  here is what the fork survey that found developer.apple.com's own vendor
  bundle corrected. One real, narrower gap remains: no `display:contents`
  (a shadow host that sets `:host{display:contents}` still generates its own
  wrapping box rather than being layout-transparent).
  `::slotted()` and `:host-context()` are parsed as unmodelled and safely
  dropped rather than guessed at.
  **Correction to the 2026-08-30 diagnosis above, and now CLOSED (2026-09-01,
  engine#80/#81)**: github.com's permanently-expanded header nav was never a
  Shadow DOM issue — confirmed by fetching its actual markup, which contains
  no `shadowrootmode`/`attachShadow` anywhere. It was two independent bugs:
  `visibility` was completely unimplemented (fixed, engine#80 — see the
  dated log entry above), and `maxExternalSheets` (20) silently dropped the
  38th of github.com's 38 `<link rel=stylesheet>` tags, which happened to be
  the one holding the header's hide/sr-only rules (fixed, raised to 64,
  engine#81). Verified live: the header now renders as a compact nav bar,
  matching a real browser.
- **CSS `@container` queries are implemented** (2026-08-31, engine#74 +
  follow-up engine integration — see the log entry above): `container-type`/
  `container-name`/the `container` shorthand, named and unnamed lookup,
  width/height size features with the same range-comparison syntax as
  `@media`, resolved against real post-layout geometry via a bounded
  cascade↔layout convergence loop (mirroring `dynamic.go`'s JS settle loop).
  Remaining, deliberate scope cuts: `@container style(...)` ("container style
  queries", a separate/newer spec feature) is not supported and its content is
  dropped wholesale; container query length units (`cqw`/`cqh`/`cqi`/`cqb`/…)
  are a different feature and are not implemented; `container-name` compares
  as a single token only (no space-separated multiple names); the measured
  container size is the border-box, not the content box a browser uses, so a
  container with substantial border/padding could resolve a boundary
  condition slightly differently than Chrome.
- **No CSS `transform`** (2D or 3D) and no `conic-gradient`, EXCEPT
  `translate`/`translateX`/`translateY` (added 2026-09-02, engine#87, for
  pkg.go.dev's off-canvas nav drawer — see FIDELITY.md's log entry above),
  `rotate`/`rotateZ` — INCLUDING the newer, independent CSS Transforms Level
  2 `rotate` property, not just the `transform: rotate()` function (added
  round 145, 2026-09-28, engine#245, for tailwindcss.com's own diagonal
  "P3 colors" swatch-name labels — the real trigger uses the standalone
  property, which modern Tailwind compiles its `rotate-*` utilities to, not
  the function) — and (round 188, 2026-10-28, engine#324) `scale`/
  `scaleX`/`scaleY`, INCLUDING the standalone `scale` property, for
  vuejs.org's own compiled stylesheet, which sets a plain, static
  `transform:scale(.8)` on its theme-toggle switch. A pure 2D translate
  needs no real coordinate-transform machinery in paint at all; rotate and
  scale do, but reuse this engine's existing filter/opacity/mask-image
  "render to an offscreen buffer, transform it, composite back" machinery
  rather than a general transform-matrix pipeline — see `paint.paintRotated`/
  `paint.paintScaled` and `css.Style.RotateDeg`/`HasScale`'s own doc comments
  for the exact, narrow scope (neither ever combines with filter/opacity/
  mask-image on the same element, or with each other; transform-origin is
  always the default centre; a NEGATIVE scale factor, CSS's own axis-flip,
  is rejected at parse time — no confirmed trigger needs a flip). `skew`,
  `matrix`, any 3D function, and any `transform` value MIXING more than one
  of translate/rotate/scale together are all still entirely unsupported — a
  carousel positioned via `transform: translateX(...) rotate(...)` renders
  at its untransformed in-flow position, same as before any of the three
  carve-outs. Also means a `transform`/
  `perspective` on an ancestor never establishes a new containing block for an
  absolutely-positioned descendant the way the spec says it should — that
  descendant's containing block resolution climbs straight past it to the
  nearest genuinely `position:`ed ancestor (or the viewport), which happens
  to be correct whenever a `relative` wrapper is also present (the common
  real-world case, confirmed live on tailwindcss.com's 3D-transforms card,
  2026-09-02) but would be wrong for a page relying on `transform` ALONE to
  scope absolute children.
- **`mask-image`/`-webkit-mask-image` only understands a single `url()`
  mask, stretched to fill the element's own border box** (added 2026-09-02,
  engine#88, confirmed load-bearing live: Wikipedia's Vector-2022 skin
  renders its ENTIRE toolbar icon set this way). The real `mask-size`/
  `mask-position`/`mask-repeat`/`mask-origin` grammar is not modelled, and a
  gradient mask or a multi-layer `mask` shorthand is left unsupported
  entirely. As a side effect of fixing this, `background-image`/`mask-image`
  URLs pointing at an SVG document now actually decode (previously they
  silently rendered nothing, regardless of mask support — `loadOneBackground`
  only ever tried a raster decoder); an SVG source is detected either by its
  URL or by sniffing the fetched bytes, matching how `<img src=*.svg>`
  already worked.
- **`calc()` only understands pure arithmetic over lengths and bare numbers**
  (`+`/`-`/`*`/`/`, nested parens — added 2026-09-02, engine#86, for
  Tailwind v4's `calc(var(--spacing) * N)` spacing scale). A `calc()` mixing
  a percentage (or `vw`/`vh`) with an absolute length cannot be resolved for
  a WIDTH-like property (width, margin, padding, …) — a percentage there
  depends on a containing block only known at LAYOUT time, which this
  text-level, pre-layout pass has no access to. `min()`/`max()` remain
  unimplemented for the same reason (see the round-104ish decline below).
  `clamp()` is now implemented, but ONLY for `font-size` (round 190,
  engine#326, `css.parseClampFontSize`): font-size resolves percentages/`vw`
  EAGERLY, at cascade time, against the parent's font-size/the viewport
  width respectively — both already known before layout runs, unlike a
  width-like property's own containing-block percentage — so the "no
  layout-time context" wall genuinely does not apply there. `clamp()` on any
  OTHER property, and `min()`/`max()` anywhere, remain unimplemented.
- **The CSS-wide `inherit` keyword only works for the properties this engine
  already gives explicit inheritance semantics to** (`color`, `visibility`,
  `font-weight`, `text-align`, `white-space`, `line-height`,
  `list-style-type`, `list-style-position` — added 2026-09-02, engine#86,
  confirmed load-bearing live: Tailwind's/most frameworks' `a{color:inherit}`
  preflight reset). `inherit` on any other property (e.g.
  `background-color:inherit`, which is not even inherited by default per
  spec) is silently dropped rather than resolved. `initial`, `unset`, and
  `revert` are not implemented at all.
- **`@supports` recognises exactly one feature test — `(color: light-dark(...))`, with or without a leading `not` — and drops every other condition's body wholesale (round 78, 2026-09-21, engine#169; `css/supports.go`'s `supportsConditionHolds`).** This entry used to say `@supports` was "entirely unimplemented," which stopped being true three weeks before this "Known gaps" section's own "updated 2026-08-30" header claimed currency — a self-referential staleness caught the same way round 141 caught the `@font-face` one: checking this section against the CURRENT code (`css/parse.go`'s `@supports` branch calls `supportsConditionHolds`) rather than trusting its own prose. The one condition it answers is exactly the postcss-preset-env `light-dark()` polyfill pattern real CSS ships (confirmed live on developer.mozilla.org, see round 78's own log entry above) — this engine has real native `light-dark()` (`css/lightdark.go`), so it can honestly say so. Still a narrow, honest answer for one capability, not a general feature-query evaluator: a property/value pair this engine does not specifically recognise, a `selector()` query, or any other condition still drops the whole block, unchanged from before round 78. A page relying on `@supports` for anything else — picking between two INCOMPATIBLE rulesets with no working fallback — still renders however the always-false answer happens to fall.
- **`html[data-theme]`-style CSS toggles depend on `@media
  (prefers-color-scheme:*)` matching optimistically for BOTH `light` and
  `dark`** (`mediaMatches` has no dedicated case for this feature, so it
  falls through to "assume applies" for either value) — cascade order alone
  then decides which wins, which happens to match "assume dark" only when the
  dark block is declared after the light one, not by any actual preference
  model. This has been correct on every real page measured so far (a `dark`
  block declared last is the overwhelmingly common source order for a
  progressive-enhancement dark-mode addition) but is not a genuine feature
  match — treat it as coincidental, not load-bearing, if it stops working on
  some future page.
- **HN's title row is ~3px shorter than Chrome's (round 149, 2026-10-04): 16px of content against Chrome's 19px at zero padding.** Measured with HN's stylesheet inlined: story pitch 36px here against Chrome's 35px, but only because a 1px UA cell padding is adding back what the title row lacks. The likely source is the vote-arrow cell (`<center><a><div class="votearrow">`), which sets the row's height in Chrome. Not yet isolated. `cellpadding="0"` (HN, all four tables, which is spec-correct) must land only after this is fixed: with the row-height fix in place, adding cellpadding alone takes HN to 1117px, 100px short of Chrome.
- **Table `colspan`/`rowspan` and `border-collapse` — re-verified (round 147, 2026-09-30): `colspan` IS implemented (`layout/table.go`'s `cellColSpan`/`tableRow.colSpan`); `rowspan` is NOT implemented anywhere (grepped the whole repo, zero hits) but no confirmed real-world trigger has been found yet — checked en.wikipedia.org's Go article and developer.mozilla.org's CSS page live, zero `rowspan` uses in either; `border-collapse` is entirely unmodelled (every table is implicitly always `separate`), and IS real on pkg.go.dev's own compiled CSS (`table{border-collapse:collapse}`, its `.UnitDirectories-table`) — but its own `td{border-bottom:var(--border)}` rule (only `border-bottom`, never `border-top`, combined with `border-spacing:0` already applied unconditionally) means collapse vs. separate produces no visible doubled-border artifact on this one confirmed trigger, a likely genuine no-op rather than a hidden defect.** Revisit `rowspan`/`border-collapse` if a future corpus page shows either one load-bearing.
- **Table-cell `vertical-align` now has real positional effect for `top`/`middle`/`bottom` (round 147, 2026-09-30, engine#248; `layout/table.go`), and a real UA default of `middle` (not the property's own general initial value, `baseline`) — confirmed load-bearing live on news.ycombinator.com's own top-nav table.** What's still NOT modelled, narrower than the full property: `baseline`'s real browser behaviour (aligning every cell's own first line baseline across the row) — this engine's current `baseline`/unset default is really "top," a pre-existing approximation unchanged by this round; and `text-top`/`text-bottom`/`sub`/`super` have no effect on a table cell specifically (the general, non-table-cell `VerticalAlign` doc comment already discloses `text-bottom`'s own separate, narrow inline-replaced-element scope elsewhere — a different code path entirely).
- **Fonts**: bundled Inter/Lora (+ Go Mono, regular-only) with real Bold/
  Italic/BoldItalic instances (no faux-bold/upright-italic), PLUS whatever
  `@font-face` fonts a page's own stylesheets name and this engine can fetch
  and decode (SFNT/WOFF2) — see round 141's own entry above for the shaping
  half of that story. This bullet used to say `@font-face` loading did not
  exist at all; it shipped three rounds before this "Known gaps" section's
  own "updated 2026-08-30" header, and this contradiction sat 130 lines below
  a bullet in this SAME section that already said so correctly (round 141's)
  — the two were never reconciled until now. A family this engine cannot
  fetch or decode still falls back to the bundled three, unchanged.
- **`sticky` ≈ `relative`, approximate z-index stacking** — the deliberate
  simplifications from Phase 1.8, unchanged.
- **CLOSED (round 178, 2026-10-22, engine#305) — SVG rasterisation cost scaled with output pixel area and was unbounded.** Deferred twice as a decision needing a human call (capping resolution OR time both change rendering output, not just internal performance — not something to pick unilaterally); asked, and told to cap output resolution. `maxSVGRasterPixels` (`svg.go`) bounds the AREA `svgToBitmap` actually rasterises at once — separate from the pre-existing `maxSVGDim` per-axis clamp, which did nothing for this case (1158×598 is nowhere near either axis's 4096 limit). Past the budget, the SVG rasterises at a reduced resolution (same aspect ratio) and the function's reported display size is UNCHANGED — only the actual bitmap shrinks, the same shape a differently-sized background image or `<img>` already has, so paint's existing `scaleTile`-based upscale (already exercised on every such mismatch) stretches it to full size at paint time with no new paint code. Deliberately NOT applied to the separately exported `RasterizeSVG` (used by `pdfcanvas`/`officecanvas`'s print-quality vector export, #229): that function explicitly promises the exact size asked for, and an export job's own time budget is not a screen render's. The original two real-world triggers (docs.docker.com's `dot-pattern.svg`, foundation.zurb.com) could not be re-measured directly — both pages have since changed and no longer serve the originally-profiled asset — so a synthetic, calibrated repro (20 stacked radial-gradient layers over a 1158×598 viewBox, matching the original's own profiled bottleneck — `rasterx.ColorFuncImage.At`, per-pixel gradient-colour sampling — rather than its path count, confirmed separately NOT to be the cost driver for many-tiny-path SVGs) stands in: `BenchmarkSVGToBitmapGradientHeavy`/`...Uncapped`, committed as a permanent pair, measured ~250ms capped vs ~683ms uncapped (~2.7x) for this repro — bounding worst-case cost rather than eliminating it, since a sufficiently expensive per-pixel SVG could still cost noticeably more than an ordinary icon even at the reduced budget.

## Reproduce

```
go run ./cmd/render -url https://example.com/ -out testdata/renders/example.com.png -w 1024 -h 768
go run ./cmd/render -url "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Go_(programming_language)" -out testdata/renders/wikipedia_go.png -w 1024 -h 768 -timeout 90s
go run ./cmd/render -url "https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc1866.html" -out testdata/renders/rfc1866.png -w 1024 -h 768 -timeout 90s
go run ./cmd/render -file testdata/floats_demo.html -out testdata/renders/floats_demo.png -w 900 -h 700
go run ./cmd/render -file testdata/flex_table_demo.html -out testdata/renders/flex_table_demo.png -w 1000 -h 700
go run ./cmd/render -file testdata/flex_wrap_demo.html -out testdata/renders/flex_wrap_demo.png -w 900 -h 700
go run ./cmd/render -file testdata/grid_demo.html -out testdata/renders/grid_demo.png -w 1000 -h 950
go run ./cmd/render -file testdata/tailwind_hero_demo.html -out testdata/renders/tailwind_hero_demo.png -w 1024 -h 620
go run ./cmd/render -file testdata/position_demo.html -out testdata/renders/position_demo.png -w 400 -h 600
go run ./cmd/render -file testdata/dropdown_hover_demo.html -out testdata/renders/dropdown_hover_demo.png -w 400 -h 300
go run ./cmd/render -file testdata/modern_css_demo.html -out testdata/renders/modern_css_demo.png -w 400 -h 300
go run ./cmd/render -file testdata/inline_decoration.html -out testdata/renders/inline_decoration.png -w 400 -h 200
```

The Phase-1.9 golden test (`go test -run TestModernCSSDemoGolden`, regenerate
with `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1`) pins the modern-colour / border-radius / dark-variant
pixels of `testdata/modern_css_demo.html` against `testdata/golden/`.

The three live URLs were reachable from this environment; no offline substitution
was needed. The two demo fixtures render fully offline via `-file`.

## Visual-fidelity protocol vs Chromium (`scripts/compare/`)

Typography and layout are checked against a real browser with a reproducible
side-by-side, not by eye alone:

```
scripts/compare/chromium-compare.sh [-w WIDTH] [-o OUTDIR] [page.html ...]
```

It renders each page (those in `scripts/compare/pages/`, or any passed
explicitly) with **go-webengine** and with **headless Chromium** at the *same*
viewport width and device scale 1, then writes a labelled `NAME.sidebyside.png`
(OURS | CHROMIUM) and prints a coarse mean-absolute pixel difference per page.

This is a **local developer tool** — it shells out to a Chrome/Chromium binary
and is deliberately **not** a CI gate (CI has no browser). The MAD score is a
guide for tracking regressions/improvements run to run, never expected to reach
0 (font rasterisers differ between engines). Regenerate the committed
`testdata/golden/*.png` with `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 go test ./...` when a change is a
verified improvement.
